[brief] F1 high sec 9.3 M1 / 9.4 G2 VERDICT CONFIRMED
CLAIM: If HOLD or SERVED works, the M1 stalled-leg count goes up. M1 counts only completed legs, so every drop the hold turns into a rescue, and every isolation release SERVED lets finish, arrives as a new completed leg that is already stalled. The brief says 'stalled exit legs FALL'. The draft quietly exempts HOLD/SERVED from that line ('they are not expected to move stalls'), never says their success pushes the count the wrong way, and still applies G2's 'strictly lower ... not higher in any single set' to clA, which includes both.
EVIDENCE: Draft 9.3 M1: 'A leg = (unit, victim) exit_start -> completion'. Draft 9.4: 'HOLD / SERVED arms: the none-longer rule applies; they are not expected to move stalls'. Draft 3.3 hold-alone replay against record wf1_results.txt:1226-1236 and 1254-1256: uhD 1291443119 pickup 84 at (41,33), d=8, completes 163 = 79 steps > 13; fmDRY 101 pickup 100 at (35,14), d=14, completes 150 = 50 > 19; f2cEFS 404 pickup 104 at (40,10), d=9, completes 146-152 = 42-48 > 14. All three predicted conversions are stalled legs. D3: record :796 says all 9 released carries were stalled carries of at least 30 steps.
FIX(reviewer): Compute G2 FALL only over legs completed in BOTH arms. Report converted legs, and legs broken in clC but completed in the arm, on their own line. For HOLD/SERVED and for clA, pre-register that raw M1 goes up by the number of conversions. Add a decision item saying the brief's literal 'stalled exit legs FALL' cannot apply to HOLD/SERVED, and what replaces it.
VERIFIER: I tried to refute each part of the claim against the draft, the record and the diagnosis. Every part holds.

1. M1 counts completed legs only. Draft :754-756 defines a leg as exit_start to completion by that unit. The census M1 copies is also conditioned on completion (exitstall_part1.txt:319-320, :345-347). Today a drop or an isolation release is therefore not an M1 leg at all. None of the 22 replacements reached a dropped victim, and all 24 dropped victims died (wf1_results.txt:1284-1286). So in clC these carries never become completed legs.

2. A HOLD conversion arrives already stalled. The record at wf1_results.txt:1226, :1230 and :1234 gives the pickups:
- uhD 1291443119: pickup 84 at (41,33), exit (49,33), d=8, threshold 13.
- fmDRY 101: pickup 100 at (35,14), exit (49,14), d=14, threshold 19.
- f2cEFS 404: pickup 104 at (40,10), exit (49,10), d=9, threshold 14.

At the drop these legs were already 64, 35 and 29 steps long, so they are stalled whatever happens after the hold. The hold replays (:1254-1256) complete them at 163, 150 and 146-152, giving legs of 79, 50 and 42-48 steps. uhD is the U30 stock control, and clC is pre-registered to equal it (draft :720). So clH on U30 is expected to show clC + 1 stalled legs, not "no move".

3. A SERVED conversion is stalled by construction. The marking needs a 30-step streak that starts at pickup (30 of 32 markings came exactly at pickup + 29, draft :150-151). On 50x50, d <= 24, so d + 5 <= 29. Any carry that survives the release and completes is therefore stalled. The record agrees: all 9 released carries were stalled carries of at least 30 steps (:796). The draft's own C2 (:155-157) and section 6 (:617-620, "HOLD 1 ENLARGES" the population of carries of 30 steps or more) already say this. That contradicts :814-815, "they are not expected to move stalls", which is a wrong prediction written into the pre-registration.

4. G2's strict rule does reach clA. Section 9.4 applies the gate "again for the combination" (:804). Shipping requires the combination to pass G1-G6 (:826-827). clA contains MODE 2, so G2's "strictly lower ... not higher in any single set" applies to it. The margins are thin. The replay predicts ugKD (U30 CRN, the shipped config) goes from 3 to 2 stalled legs (:344). One or two conversions in a single set of clA can therefore cancel mode 2's gain or push the count higher. That would be a FAIL caused by rescues.

5. The brief's gate line is narrowed without saying so. The brief asks that "stalled exit legs FALL" for each fix. G2 is labelled "(MODE arms)", and no decision item in section 11 records that HOLD and SERVED cannot meet this line.

6. The metric also departs from the diagnosis. The diagnosis's recommended gate (exitstall_part1.txt:690) was "stalled and broken carrying legs ... must fall". The draft moved broken legs out of the gate: M2 has "no pass line of their own" (:822).

The reviewer's main fix goes too far. Restricting FALL to legs completed in both arms would make the test weak and biased. In the stock instrument, and after the first mode-2 step, runs diverge. Later pickups then fail to match on (tuple, unit, victim, pickup step, cell), so the restriction would discard most legs after the divergence, including the legs the count exists to measure. The better fix is to change which legs are counted.

Quarantine: I read only named files (the draft, wf1_results.txt, and outputs/exitstall_part1.txt through ls, grep and sed on that one file). I ran no recursive command.
FIX(verified): 1. Change what G2 counts rather than restricting it to legs completed in both arms. Count per exit_start. A leg is STALLED-OR-BROKEN if any of these holds:
   - it completed with length > d + 5;
   - it ended without completion (route_blocked drop, died, isolation release);
   - it was cut at the horizon with elapsed > d + 5.

   G2's FALL test, both pooled and per set, applies to this count. This is the diagnosis's own gate ("stalled and broken carrying legs ... must fall", exitstall_part1.txt:690) and its "with broken legs counted" census line (:345-347). A HOLD or SERVED conversion then moves a leg from broken to stalled-completed and leaves the count unchanged. A SERVED carry that now runs to the horizon instead of being released stays counted. MODE 2's reduction still shows in clA, and conversions can no longer cancel it.

2. Keep the census-comparable completed-only count (M1-primary, and M1-secondary to the completion cell) as reported lines, with no gate. Put the legs broken in clC and completed in the arm (conversions) on their own line, and exclude them from the none-longer comparison. Keep none-longer on matched legs as drafted; a leg completed in clC and broken in the arm still counts as longer.

3. Replace "they are not expected to move stalls" (:814-815) with the true prediction: under completed-only counting, every HOLD or SERVED conversion adds one stalled completed leg.
   - SERVED: by construction, since the marking needs 30 steps and d + 5 <= 29 on 50x50.
   - HOLD: all three recorded convertible legs were already 64, 35 and 29 steps long at the drop, against thresholds of 13, 19 and 14.
   - Pre-register raw M1 in clH, clSN, clSB and clA as rising by the number of conversions (M3 completed plus custody carries that complete).

4. Single HOLD and SERVED arms: pre-register "stalled-or-broken not higher (pooled and per set), and none longer", plus their mechanism metric (M3 conversions > 0, or M4 custody markings -> 0).

5. Add decision item D-7. It should state that the brief's literal "stalled exit legs FALL" cannot be met by HOLD or SERVED: neither shortens a leg, and success turns a broken leg into a long completed one. It should propose item 4 as the replacement test for those arms, and item 1's count for MODE arms and clA, and ask the maintainer to accept both.

[brief] F2 high sec 3.3 VERDICT PARTLY
CLAIM: The section is titled 'Does a trapped carrier still get help? YES', but the design sends no help at all. Under HOLD 1 no unit is ever dispatched toward a trapped carrier. (c) says so outright, and (d) only helps after the carrier has died, when the draft itself says the co-located victim dies in the same sweep. A carrier trapped in a pocket (neighbours not burning, no route to any boundary) never raises route_blocked and gets nothing under any switch. The brief asks for a confirmation; the draft answers by redefining 'help'.
EVIDENCE: Brief: 'confirm a trapped carrier still gets help rather than simply being left.' Draft 3.3(c): 'no second unit is pulled off whatever it is doing'. 3.3(d): 'In practice the co-located victim dies in the same sweep.' Record :1574 and :1590: under the fix 'no second unit is sent'. Draft 4.2 side effect: a carrier that never finishes keeps its victim 'assigned' to the horizon. No section covers the pocketed, non-enclosed carrier.
FIX(reviewer): Say it plainly: under HOLD 1 no help is dispatched, and the record shows 0 of 22 replacements ever re-picked up a dropped victim. Then either design a non-replacing help rung as its own arm, e.g. HOLD=2 = hold plus a support request that sends the nearest idle or firefighting unit to extinguish the burning neighbours of the held carrier's cell (no unassign, no second claimant, off by default), or put 'no help' to the maintainer as decision D-7. Also cover the pocket case, e.g. mode 2's BFS finding no fire-free path for K steps as the 'genuinely unable' signal.
VERIFIER: The facts behind the finding are right. The fix it proposes is not supported by the record.

WHAT HOLDS:
(1) Under HOLD 1 no unit is ever sent toward a held carrier. Draft 3.3(c) says so, and so do record lines 1574 and 1590 ("no second unit is sent"). The victim stays claimed, so the planner's needy filter (rescue_planner 560-562), the executor guard and _try_dispatch all skip it.
(2) Section 3.3(d) never delivers help, in every case, not just "in practice". A held carrier dies only when its own cell burns. Its victim never leaves that cell: it moves with the carrier at 1841-1847, the hold does not move, and custody blocks fleeing. The casualty sweep kills victims first, deterministically for any victim on a burning cell (wildfire_model.py:5228-5240). So no living victim can ever be re-dispatched after a held carrier dies. Point (d) is a note about state safety (the marker guard), not help.
(3) Point (c) implies the firefighters who stay on the fire "open the enclosure". The firefighting planner does not know about carriers. It works only for idle units (agents.py:2488-2492) and heads for the front cell nearest the unit itself (agents.py:2659-2668).
(4) Pocket case. An exiting unit never runs the path search (agents.py:2387-2391, "if not self.exiting"). So a pocketed carrier raises nothing, today or under any switch, and section 3.3 never mentions it. The record has one pocket: d4off east/half/1323590814, an 11-cell region for steps 129-131 (record line 805).
So the heading "YES" overstates what the design does. The brief asked for a confirmation, and the honest answer is "no dispatched help". That answer should be stated plainly and put to the maintainer.

WHAT DOES NOT HOLD:
(a) The draft does not hide this. Point (c) states it outright; only the heading reframes it. This is a gap in how the answer is framed, not a design that would behave wrongly or lose mode-0 identity. It is medium, not high.
(b) "Gets nothing under any switch" is overstated for the pocket case. Today the pocketed carrier's only ending is the isolation release at 30, which unassigns and cancels the victim (the D3 path). With SERVED 1 on, it keeps custody.
(c) The proposed HOLD=2 "support request" arm would do nothing on every recorded enclosure, and it would cost at least 146 runs:
- Enclosures lasted 2-4 steps. Class C: 132-134, 135-137, 147-149, 204-206. Class B: 168-171, when the carrier's own cell ignites. (Record table, lines 1226-1244.)
- The nearest other unit was 31-71 cells away, moving 1 cell per step, with extinguish reach 2 (agents.py:1584). It would arrive about 30 steps after the enclosure had resolved.
- 14 of 15 immediate replacements were themselves route_blocked-unassigned within 0-1 steps.
- A directed support request would also pull a firefighter off its own front, which is a real cost in the shipped configuration.
(d) The suggested pocket signal (K steps with no BFS path means "genuinely unable") would bring back the same replace-the-co-located-carrier response the record shows never worked (0 of 22 reached, 24 of 24 victims died). A unit outside a pocket cannot reach inside it by definition.
FIX(verified): Rewrite 3.3 without adding an arm:

1. New heading: "3.3 DOES A TRAPPED CARRIER STILL GET HELP? NOT DISPATCHED HELP. None that could arrive in time exists today either."

2. First line: under HOLD 1 no unit is dispatched toward a held carrier. The victim stays claimed, so the planner, the executor guard and _try_dispatch skip it.

3. Keep (a), and add why dispatched help is too late:
- recorded enclosures lasted 2-4 steps (132-134, 135-137, 147-149, 204-206, 168-171);
- the nearest other unit was 31-71 cells away, moving 1 cell per step, with extinguish reach 2 (agents.py:1584);
- the firefighting planner heads for the front nearest the unit itself (agents.py:2659-2668) and has no notion of a carrier.
Remove the implication in (c) that firefighters "open the enclosure".

4. Recast (d) as a state-safety note, not help. A held carrier dies only when its own cell burns, and its co-located victim is always swept first (wildfire_model.py:5228-5240), so no living victim is ever re-dispatched. Change "in practice" to "always".

5. Add the pocket case. An exiting unit never runs the path search (agents.py:2388), so a pocketed carrier raises nothing today and nothing under any switch.
- Today its only ending is the isolation release at 30. One pocket is recorded: d4off east/half/1323590814, 129-131.
- With SERVED 1 on, it keeps custody.
- With mode 2 on, it falls back per 2.3.3.
- Replacing a co-located carrier is not help.

6. Add decision D-7 to sections 0 and 11: "The brief asks to confirm a trapped carrier gets help. Under HOLD 1 none is dispatched. I recommend accepting this, because dispatched help arrives about 30 steps after every recorded enclosure resolved. If you want a help rung anyway, it would be a separate, default-off HOLD=2 arm: a support request to extinguish the held cell's burning neighbours, with no unassign and no second claimant. It is predicted to do nothing on the recorded population and costs about 146 more runs."

Do not put HOLD=2 into the default Part 2/3 plan.

VERIFIER: The core of the finding holds against the record at this source. Part of the proposed fix does not hold, and is refined below.

Evidence: my own scan, review/verify_f3/scan.py. It runs a non-recursive listdir of outputs/, skips the quarantine by name, and reads only _ffr_<tag>_*.json.

1. SERVED never triggers in the shipped configuration at this source.
   - ugD 57, uhD 30, ugKD 30, ugmD 1, mgD 3 and ugX 23 (144 runs) have 0 geographically_isolated markings of a victim bound to an exiting unit.
   - The gated control at this source (ugC 57, uhC 30, ugKC 30) also has 0.
   - The longest run of carrying steps with zero other reachability starts is 15 steps in the shipped config and 14 in the control. The marking needs 29; recorded triggers such as d4off 1323590814, ihbasefresh 606/1010 and dfpON west/505 each show exactly 29.
   - All 9 D3 cases the draft cites (C1, section 4.3, D-1) come from non-shipped configs at older sources: dim*, ih*, lat*, ph*, vmon3, dimfresh*, d4off, dfp*, f2cDRY, f2b404. The draft never says the shipped record has 0.
   - SERVED changes state only through geo_streaks (wildfire_model.py:5085-5097), and those are observable only at a marking (5121). The streak resets whenever the victim is served (rescue_planner.py:499-502). So clSN is value-identical to clC on every tuple where clC has no custody marking.
   - clSN therefore passes G1-G6 without acting once. The SHIPPING rule (draft 826-829) does not use the 'inert' label, so SERVED would ship.

2. HOLD triggers only a handful of times.
   - Stock C13: ugD east/half/101 @135. The carrier dies in the same step (class A), so the hold cannot convert it.
   - Stock U30: uhD 1291443119 @148, the only convertible event (class C).
   - CRN U30 to step 240 (ugKD): 0 drops.
   - So M3, whose primary instrument is CRN (draft line 768), has no known convertible trigger in CRN.

3. The mechanism tests are true by construction.
   - At HOLD 1 the only carrier raise site (2411) becomes the hold. "M2 drops -> 0" is therefore guaranteed whenever clC has drops, and vacuous when clC has none.
   - At SERVED >= 1, "M4 custody markings -> 0" is guaranteed.
   - Neither test can report an ineffective fix, and a result of 0 -> 0 is undefined.

Overstatements in the finding, and where its fix goes wrong:
- HOLD will certainly act: clC reproduces ugD/uhD (per the draft's own cross-checks), so clH diverges on at least 2 tuples.
- The proposed mechanism population is weak.
  - D-4 as drafted has only clOC/clOA and no single arms, so it cannot credit a single fix.
  - The mechanic-OFF-like control at this source (ugC/uhC/ugKC, 117 runs) shows 0 SERVED triggers and 1 class-A HOLD trigger.
  - "Recorded D3 tuples in their recorded configs" cannot be run as stated. Those configs belong to older sources (pre-dimfix ih/dim, d4off BASE_STATION_MODE 0 at 2026-09-13, the dfp worktree with west wind, f2b404 probes), so recurrence at 6160438 is not guaranteed.

Severity stays high. As written, SERVED would be unmeasurable in Part 3 yet ship, which defeats the brief's "so Part 3 can credit each one".

FIX(verified): 1. Pre-register the expected triggers from the record at this source, in 9.4 and in sections 3.3 and 4.

   - HOLD, stock U30 at 360: 1 known trigger, east/half/1291443119 @148. It is class C, and the replay converts it at 163.
   - HOLD, CRN U30 to step 240: 0.
   - SERVED: 0 in the 144 shipped-config runs and the 117 control runs. The longest zero-other-start carry is 15 steps, against the 29 a marking needs.
   - Say plainly in C1, 4.3 and D-1 that all 9 D3 cases and the 19 co-marked victims come from non-shipped configurations at older sources.

2. Count triggers per single-fix arm with no new instrument. HOLD and SERVED change behaviour only at their trigger, so:
   - A tuple where the arm is value-identical to clC is a tuple where the fix never acted.
   - Report the first differing step, and the clC event at that step (a carrier drop or a custody marking). Classify each HOLD trigger A/B/C/D.
   - M3 is then reported per trigger: converted / died in custody / isolation / horizon.

3. Replace the inert rule.

   - Zero triggers on a set means UNTESTED on that set, never PASS.
   - Add to SHIPPING: a fix whose triggers across all sets and instruments are 0, or are all of a class the fix cannot convert, does not ship on the gate. It may ship only as "safe by construction, effect unmeasured" with the maintainer's explicit yes. Make this a new decision, D-7.

[brief] F4 high sec 7 / 9.1 / 9.6 VERDICT CONFIRMED
CLAIM: Kill-switch identity is proven only before the flip, at the defaults (cfv = 0). The commit that ships changes the cfv default, the accessor's missing-value fallback and possibly the junk mapping. The switch as it ships (an explicit --set X=0 overriding a non-zero default) is never measured against 6160438; the flip commit re-runs only the suite.
EVIDENCE: Brief: 'Kill switches, with value-identity against 6160438 proven when all are off.' Draft 7: 'A passing fix is flipped in a final, separate commit that changes its cfv default, its accessor's missing-value fallback, the junk mapping ... and re-runs the suite'. Draft 9.6 step 4 ends 'the flip' with no identity arm.
FIX(reviewer): At the flip commit, run C13 stock at 360 with --set FF_EXIT_LEG_MODE=0 --set FF_EXIT_LEG_HOLD=0 --set FF_EXIT_LEG_SERVED=0 and compare against clB, same field set as 9.1, 13/13. STOP on any difference. That is 13 runs. Add it to 9.5 and 9.6.
VERIFIER: The finding holds against the draft text, and the code shows a concrete way the gap could fail.

1. Draft text. Section 9.1 measures identity only as "clC vs clB", where clC is "the branch at its defaults" (5.2). At that point every cfv default is 0 (sections 0 and 7), so the default and the kill value are the same thing. Section 7 says the flip commit changes source: the cfv default, the accessor's missing-value fallback, possibly the junk mapping, and the pinning tests. It then "re-runs the suite" and nothing else. Section 9.6 step 4 ends with "the flip" and has no identity arm. Neither 9.5 nor 9.7 has an identity measurement after the flip. The shipped kill switch is `--set X=0` overriding a non-zero default, and it is never run.

2. Why the Part 3 check cannot stand in for it. agents.py:10 does `from common_fixed_variables import *`. apply_scenario_config (src_extension/adaptation/local_adaptation_generator.py:270-273) sets attributes only on cfv and wildfire_model, not on agents. agents.py:903-910 records that a star-imported or import-time read makes a switch "silently inert", and that this dead-input defect has hit this repo nine times. Suppose one of the new call sites slips and reads a bare name, for example at the 1840 carrying-move site or the 2411 hold site.
   - At default 0, the Part 3 clC-vs-clB identity still passes, because the frozen value is also 0.
   - After the flip, that site holds the shipped value. `--set FF_EXIT_LEG_MODE=0` never reaches it, so the kill switch leaks.
   - The 8.2 unit tests check read-at-call-time for the accessor, not for each call site. The suite therefore does not close this gap. Only a run with a non-zero default and an explicit 0 can expose it.
   - The harness's _parse_value (outputs/_ffr_harness.py:73-87) turns "0" into int 0, so such an arm does exercise the exact-zero path.

3. Project precedent requires measuring at the commit that ships.
   - Ungated flip: outputs/ungated_report.txt:40 "G1 flip == explicitly configured arm ... PASS 23/23".
   - Depot-fireproof, shipped ON: outputs/depotfireproof_report.txt:126-128 ran dfpOFF (the feature checkout with =0) against dfpB and got 13/13. At :155-159, when "THE SOURCE MOVED AFTER THE WAVE", it was re-measured.
   - The flip round compared no-set runs with explicit-set runs (flip-round memory, _flip3_compare.py).
   The draft's flip commit changes source after the wave and measures nothing, so it breaks the project's own rule.

4. Severity. The by-construction argument in 5.2 does still hold for a correct transcription, since the exact-zero path does not depend on the default. So this is a missing proof rather than a known break. It still meets the high bar: the brief's gate ("kill switches prove value-identity") would be certified for a commit other than the one that ships, and that commit's own kill path is exactly where this repo's most frequent defect class would hide.

5. The proposed fix is right but covers only half the problem. It proves the OFF side of the flipped commit. It does not prove that the flipped default actually runs the configuration Part 3 measured. A fallback or junk-mapping slip, or a default that silently fails to take effect, would ship an unmeasured configuration. The ungated and flip rounds closed that with a no-set vs explicit-set identity check. That costs 13 more runs with no new control, because the explicit-set C13 stock 360 runs already exist from Part 3.
FIX(verified): Add a post-flip identity step to 9.1, 9.5, 9.6 step 4 and 9.7. Run it on the flip commit itself, after the suite and before the round is reported.

(a) KILL SWITCH AS SHIPPED. Run the canonical 13 (C13), stock, 360 steps (--steps 360 --set BATCH_SIZE=360 --uav-actions), with --set FF_EXIT_LEG_MODE=0 --set FF_EXIT_LEG_HOLD=0 --set FF_EXIT_LEG_SERVED=0. Set all three explicitly, including any that did not ship. Compare against clB (6160438 worktree) over every recorded field except {tag, repo, wall_s, params, extra_params}, plus stdout_sha256. Requirement: 13/13 value-identical. That is 13 runs.

(b) SHIPPED DEFAULT = MEASURED ARM. Run C13, stock, 360 steps, with no FF_EXIT_LEG_* --set. Compare against the Part 3 C13 stock runs of the arm whose configuration is being shipped, using the same field set. That arm is the passing single arm if only one fix ships, otherwise clA or the re-run passing-subset combination arm. Requirement: 13/13 value-identical. That is 13 runs, and the reference already exists. This is the ungated round's G1 "flip == explicitly configured arm" and the flip round's no-set vs explicit-set check.

STOP on any difference in either check, and do not report the flip as done. Add 26 runs to the 9.5 budget. Use fresh, unused tags for both arms (check that no _ffr_<tag>_* file exists or is tracked). The analyzer must refuse any run whose repo, extra_params or steps are not its arm's.

[brief] F6 medium sec 2.3.1 / 0 / 11 VERDICT PARTLY
CLAIM: Mode 2 departs from the brief's definition without asking. The brief proposes a 'fire-free search path'. The draft uses a CLEAN search: not burning, not fire-adjacent, not smoky, and the goal must be clean too. So a carrier with a fire-free path but no clean one gets today's rule, i.e. it keeps stalling. The departure is explained in 2.3.1 but is missing from the decision list D-1..D-6. The record also warns that a clean goal keeps a refusal that protects nothing.
EVIDENCE: Brief: '2 = mode 1 plus following a fire-free search path to the nearest edge'. Draft 2.3.1: 'A cell n may be entered iff it is in bounds and CLEAN' and 'Deliberately NOT done ... a plain fire-free (burning-only) search'. Record :123-127: the clean goal 'keeps, at the last step, the refusal the diagnosis showed protects nothing'.
FIX(reviewer): Add decision D-7: 'mode 2 searches clean cells, not the brief's fire-free cells; with no clean path it behaves exactly as today'. State the answer to 'what if no fire-free path exists' separately from 'no clean path'. Optionally offer a burning-only variant as a named rung, flagged unreplayed.
VERIFIER: The finding's main claim does not hold: the draft does not depart from the brief. What is left is a wording problem, and it is low severity, not medium.

1. The brief's phrase comes from the diagnosis, and the diagnosis defines mode 2 over CLEAN cells. The brief's line "Proposed: ... 2 = mode 1 plus following a fire-free search path to the nearest edge" summarises the diagnosis's S1 proposal. That proposal, at outputs/exitstall_part1.txt:628-633, says: "mode 2 (F5 + F1): each carrying step takes the first step of a stateless breadth-first search to the nearest boundary cell. The search runs over clean cells: not burning, not fire-adjacent, not smoky, _move_toward's own clean test. With no clean route it falls back to today's rule." Its decision item D2 (:708-709) asked the maintainer to open the round on "F5+F1". The draft's 2.3.1 implements that definition exactly: the passable test, the clean goal, the start exemption and the fallback. It is also exactly the replayed kernel, fs_rules.py bfs_first_step with strict=True, called that way at fs_counterfactual.py:74-79. So there is nothing to put to the maintainer as a new decision (D-7). They approved this rule when they opened the round.

2. The brief's "no fire-free path" question is already answered. A clean path is also a fire-free path: every entered cell, the goal included, must be non-burning. So "no fire-free path" implies "no clean path", and the draft's 2.3.3 fallback covers it: the carrier runs today's _move_toward(exit_target) unchanged, including the same no-neighbour branch. It therefore cannot end up worse than today. A carrier that has a fire-free path but no clean one also gets today's rule, so the brief's "not worse" requirement holds there too.

3. "It keeps stalling" is true only as a bound, and a small one. In the replay the fallback ran on 4 of 212 legs (13 of 1643 advances). Measured to the cell actually reached, only 2 legs stayed stalled, and only one of those involved the fallback: f2cES east/half/101, with 10 fallback steps and 5 reversals (record :93-98). The other, fmEFS east/half/111, was a clean detour, not a fallback.

4. The optional burning-only rung should NOT be added. It was never replayed: nothing in outputs/_cl_replay calls bfs_first_step with strict=False. Adding it would cost a full arm (73 tuples x 2 instruments = 146 runs plus rbgate shards) for a rule with no estimate, and it would depart from the pre-registered replay numbers that the kernel check in 8.4 depends on.

5. The record's warning about the clean goal (record :123-127) is already handled. The draft's "Deliberately NOT done" list names the relaxed goal test and says it was never replayed.

What is actually wrong is the draft's own wording. Section 2.3.1 lists "a plain fire-free (burning-only) search" under "Deliberately NOT done". Because the brief uses the word "fire-free", a maintainer reading that bullet would think the draft rejected the brief's rule. The draft uses "fire-free" to mean burning-only, which matches the diagnosis's usage in S2 (:661). Neither 2.3.1 nor 2.3.3 says that the clean definition IS the diagnosis's mode-2 definition that the brief summarises, or restates the brief's "no fire-free path" case in its own terms. That costs no wave, but it can mislead the maintainer's review, so it deserves a sentence.

VERIFIER: The finding is correct, and the defect can be measured directly.

How the guard matches: _ug_seeds.py:54-59 compares OWN against the start of the lower-cased basename (`base.startswith(OWN)`). A name like `_ffr_xsTD_east_half_2070841104.json` does not start with `_xs`, `xs` or `exitstall`. That is exactly why the existing OWN lists `_ffr_ug` and `_ffr_uh` separately. The record (wf1_results.txt, around :459-466) says the fix is to add `_xs`, `_ffr_xs`, `xs`, `exitstall` and this round's prefix. Draft 8.3 (lines 680-684) shortened this to "_xs* / xs* / exitstall*" and dropped `_ffr_xs`. It also leaves out `_ffr_cl`, even though the draft's own `_cl_seeds.py:54` lists `_ffr_cl` and `_rblatch_camp2_cl`.

How I checked: I wrote a read-only scan, review/f7verify/own_variants.py. It does not import or run the selector. It lists only outputs/, outputs/_ffr_logs/ and outputs/_cl_replay/, without recursing, and adds tracked `git ls-files -- outputs` plus tracked queue files. It counts which U30 seeds the guard would still treat as "used":
- CURRENT OWN: 7 seeds hit today; 30 once this round's planned `_ffr_cl*` and `cl*` runs exist.
- DRAFT 8.3 OWN: still 7 today, every one of them from `_ffr_xsTD/TK/RAD/RED/TY/RAY/REY` names. That becomes 30 of 30 once `_ffr_clC/clD/clKC/clKD_*_<U30 seed>.json` exist. The CRN harness also writes `_ffr_<tag>_` names, as the existing `_ffr_ugKC_*` files show.
- REVIEWER OWN: 0 today and 0 after the planned runs.

Why this breaks the analyzers: each accepted U30 seed that gets hit causes its candidate to be rejected, so choose() returns a different set. `_ug_analyze._u30()` (:72-84, which runs at import) and `_ug_queue.u30()` (:50-53) then raise "U30 MISMATCH", and `_uh_analyze`/`_uh_queue` fail the same way because they import it. The draft's fix therefore does not repair U30, and this round's own U30 wave would break it again. The part about `_cl_analyze` is conditional, since that file is not written yet. But the draft says it "STOPS on any mismatch", and the draft's analyzers follow the "fresh choose() disagrees" pattern (9.2), so it would die the same way.

Is adding `cl`/`xs` too broad? No. At declaration, no U30 candidate was rejected as "used" (the 2 rejections were on ignition cell), so skipping more names cannot change the chosen set.

Severity: medium is right. The failure is loud (a STOP, not a wrong number), but it blocks analysis of one of the three required seed sets and would cost a re-run of the tooling.

Quarantine: my commands listed the top level of outputs/, which shows the quarantine entry's name. My script skips it by name and never enters it. The grep/ls pipeline also filtered it out by name. git ls-files was tracked-only (no --others). Nothing traversed it.

[brief] F9 medium sec 3.2 / 3.3(c) VERDICT PARTLY
CLAIM: The hold branch drops a recommendation the verification record made: apply the 2460-2463 tail relabel when the carrier is held. A carrier that began its carry already labelled route_blocked, and then holds before its first move, stays invisible to _find_active_firefighter_for_victim. For that case 3.3(c)'s 'the victim stays CLAIMED' is false: a double claim becomes possible, and so does the A8 'carried out but scored unreachable' hazard.
EVIDENCE: Record :1636-1638: 'Recommendation: in the fix's hold branch, apply the tail's relabel when exiting. Do not remove the tail.' Draft 3.2 hold code: `if self.exiting and ff_exit_leg_hold(): self._last_move_tier = 0; return` with no status handling.
FIX(reviewer): In the hold branch, if status == 'route_blocked', set status = 'assigned' (mirroring 2460-2463), under HOLD != 0 only. Add a unit test: a route_blocked exiting unit that holds becomes visible to the lookup. Or say explicitly why it is left out (0 recorded).
VERIFIER: The finding is right about the omission and the false claim. It is wrong to say the hold makes the hazard possible: the hazard exists today and the hold leaves it unchanged.

What holds:
1. The draft drops the record's recommendation and never says why.
   - The record says so explicitly (wf1_results.txt:1592, 1636-1638): "The one exception: a carrier that began its carry already labelled route_blocked and then holds before its first move" and "Recommendation: in the fix's hold branch, apply the tail's relabel when exiting."
   - The draft's hold code in 3.2 (draft:391-396) has no status handling.
   - The draft mentions neither the tail nor the exception anywhere except for the mode-2 path step (draft:290-291).
2. The state the finding describes can occur at 6160438.
   - Pickup (agents.py:1781-1793) sets exiting=True and exit_target, with no move and no status write.
   - A unit can therefore start its carry already labelled route_blocked. Two ways:
     - A second-raise approaching unit keeps its task (the handler returns early; the docstring of _release_other_claimants, wildfire_model.py:3757-3764). It then reaches the target through _assigned_one_step_retreat (agents.py:2079/2095/2130/2137 -> 2291-2315), which moves it without relabelling.
     - The victim moves onto the unit's cell and _refresh_target_from_victim (agents.py:1700-1720) retargets it.
   - The only thing that clears the label is _move_toward's tail (agents.py:2460-2463), which runs only on a successful move. A hold before the first move therefore keeps the unit route_blocked.
   - Such a unit is invisible to _find_active_firefighter_for_victim, which skips it at wildfire_model.py:3739-3741.
3. So 3.3(c)'s universal "The victim stays CLAIMED" (draft:446-450) is false for this case.
   - This is internally inconsistent: 3.2 rejects the "return early" handler variant precisely because a carrier held while labelled route_blocked is invisible and leads to A8 (draft:410-415).
   - The design leaves exactly that state standing for a carrier that was already labelled.

What is overstated:
- "A double claim becomes possible, and so does the A8 hazard" suggests the hold creates the risk. It does not.
  - At HOLD 0 today, an already-route_blocked enclosed carrier calls _mark_route_blocked. That is a no-op, because the status is already route_blocked (agents.py:2355-2356). No handler, no unassign: it already holds custody while invisible.
  - The draft's HOLD 1 branch does the same thing, differing only in the _last_move_tier label.
  - The hazard is pre-existing and not worsened. It is recorded 0 times: second_raise.py found 0 end-of-step ff_steps rows with exiting=True and status route_blocked in 2513 runs (row layout (ff, pos, status, assigned, exiting, dead), confirmed at _ffr_harness.py:30/648).
- The A8 half is covered in every arm with SERVED >= 1, because the draft's custody predicate deliberately has no status filter (draft:522-524). It stays open only in the clH arm (hold on, served off).
- The relabel also cannot cover the pickup step itself: that step ends with the carrier still route_blocked. A universal "stays claimed" still needs a caveat.

Severity: low to medium. The design text is wrong and silent, but the case is pre-existing, never recorded, and not changed by the fix.
FIX(verified): Adopt the record's recommendation, and state the edge case honestly.

(1) Relabel in the hold branch at agents.py:2411, only when HOLD is not 0:
    if not scored:
        if self.exiting and ff_exit_leg_hold():
            if str(getattr(self, "status", "") or "").strip().lower() == "route_blocked":
                self.status = "assigned" if self.assigned else "available"   # mirrors the tail at 2460-2463

This does not change mode-0 identity, because the whole branch is inside the switch.

[brief] F10 medium sec 9.4 G2 (none longer) VERDICT CONFIRMED
CLAIM: 'None gets longer' is checked only on matched legs (same tuple, instrument, unit, victim, pickup step and cell). Once a run diverges, which the record says any change in completion timing causes (absence-draw reordering, fire-stream shift), every later leg is unmatched and falls outside the check. The gate can pass while longer legs go uncounted, and the draft does not report how many legs the check covers.
EVIDENCE: Brief GATE: 'stalled exit legs FALL and none gets longer'. Draft 9.4: 'over matched legs (same tuple, instrument, unit, victim, pickup step and cell)'. Record :1135-1138: changing when completions happen reorders absence draws, and the shared fire stream shifts.
FIX(reviewer): Report matched coverage (matched / all legs) per arm and set. For unmatched legs, pre-register a set-level check, e.g. maximum excess and the count of legs with excess > 5 no higher than clC. Or state that the gate covers matched legs only and add that to D-3.
VERIFIER: The finding holds against the draft and the recorded data. The reviewer's proposed fix is partly redundant, so a refined fix is given below.

1. The draft text is as the reviewer says. Section 9.4 G2 applies "none longer" only "over matched legs (same tuple, instrument, unit, victim, pickup step and cell)". Its first half, the stalled count, is set-level over all legs. D-3 covers only matched legs whose fire diverged after pickup. Nothing in 9.3 or 9.4 reports how many legs are matched, or what happens to legs that have no counterpart. The draft also leans on this check at 2.3.3: "What this does NOT prove: that the TRAJECTORY is never worse ... That is what the gate measures (... matched-leg lengths ...)". The key includes the pickup step, so a leg picked up after the run diverges almost never matches. Those downstream legs are exactly where a worse trajectory would show: a unit freed earlier carries a different victim under different fire. So the draft's claim that matched-leg lengths catch a worse trajectory is wrong for downstream legs. Within a single leg it does hold, because a mid-leg divergence keeps the same pickup.

2. The record confirms the mechanism. wf1_results.txt :1135-1138 says that changing when completions happen reorders the absence draws. With the mechanic ON, the shared fire stream also shifts. CRN does not prevent the unmatching. _fm2_probe_harness.py:34-40 and :115-117 pin only Fire.step's draw per (seed, cell id, steps_counter), not the dedicated absence stream (_ff_absence_rng) or dispatch timing, so pickup steps still move under CRN. One overstatement: "every later leg" is not strictly true, because another unit's leg can still coincide on pickup step and cell. That does not change the finding.

3. I measured the size of the gap on recorded runs (read-only script: scratchpad/review/verify_f10/coverage.py, cov2.py, cov3.py). An unconstrained leg's normal excess over d is exactly 1: uhD 97/103, ugKD 93/96. So a MODE arm can first change a run only at a leg with excess > 1, or at a broken leg. Legs picked up after the pickup of the first such leg are an upper bound on the legs a MODE arm leaves unmatched:
   - uhD 3/103, ugKD 2/96, uhC 1/106, ugKC 5/96
   - sfON 2/73, fmEFS 1/84, f2cEFS 6/74, dfD 1/69, flhD 1/61

   That is 1-8% of legs, about 1-6 per arm. Stalls tend to be the last carry in a run. On top of these come NEW legs in the feature arm with no clC counterpart, such as a converted drop or a victim reached because a unit came free earlier.

   So matched coverage should be high for the MODE arms. But the uncovered legs are precisely the downstream ones, and nobody has measured or stated the coverage. The only protection for those legs is G2's set-level stalled count and G3's rescued count. A downstream leg that goes from excess 8 to excess 80, or from 1 to 5, passes the gate unseen. Severity: medium-low rather than medium. It will not cost a wave, but it can mislead the report and makes 2.3.3 overclaim.

4. Problems with the reviewer's fix. The proposed "count of legs with excess > 5 no higher than clC" duplicates G2 as it stands, which already says "not higher in any single set" for the stalled count over all legs. The coverage reporting and a set-level rule for unmatched legs are the parts that add something.
FIX(verified): 1. In 9.3 M1, add MATCHED COVERAGE per arm, per seed set and per instrument: the number of matched legs over all clC legs, and over all feature-arm legs. Also record each tuple's first divergence step, taken from ff_steps and fire_digests (the _ffr_analyze first-divergence method). Every leg can then be split into PRE-divergence legs (these must match; any unmatched one is an analyzer defect, so STOP) and POST-divergence legs.

2. For post-divergence and unmatched legs in each arm, report the count, broken count, total excess, max excess and number stalled. Show these side by side with clC's post-divergence legs on the same tuples, and list the feature arm's legs that have no clC counterpart separately.

3. In 9.4 G2, state explicitly that "none longer" is enforced per leg on matched legs only. Unmatched and downstream legs are bounded only by the set-level stalled count (already in G2) and by G3. Fold this into D-3 so the maintainer chooses between two options:
   - (a) accept that with coverage reported;
   - (b) pre-register a set-level rule for post-divergence legs: their max excess and total excess are not higher than clC's post-divergence legs on any set and instrument, and any exceedance is reported with its cause.

   Do not add a separate count of legs with excess > 5: G2's per-set "not higher" stalled rule already covers it.

4. Correct 2.3.3. The line "That is what the gate measures (... matched-leg lengths ...)" should say that matched-leg lengths cover the same leg only. Downstream legs are covered by the coverage report and the rule chosen under D-3.

5. Pre-register the expected coverage from the recorded runs: legs after the first changeable leg are 1-8% of all legs (uhD 3/103, ugKD 2/96, f2cEFS 6/74). A MODE arm whose coverage falls far below that has diverged earlier than the design predicts, and that should be explained before it is credited.

[hold] D2-1 high sec 3.3(e), 6, 9.4 SHIPPING VERDICT PARTLY
CLAIM: The design's guarantee that a held carrier still gets help ('rather than simply being left') holds only when FF_EXIT_LEG_SERVED is also on. Yet the ship rule lets HOLD ship alone. With HOLD alone, the hold itself runs the carried victim's isolation streak on every held step. At 30 the escape unassigns the held carrier, then marks the victim unreachable and cancelled, with no replacement ever attempted. That is exactly 'simply being left', and today's drop resets the streak where the hold feeds it.

[hold] D2-3 medium sec 3.3, 9.3 VERDICT PARTLY
CLAIM: 'A trapped carrier still gets help' is argued, not measured. Part 3 has no hold census, and a hold cannot be seen in any exported field. The draft never states that a hold is bounded, or how a hold can end. D2's own mechanism is observed only through M3 matches to clC drops, which misses every hold on a diverged trajectory.
EVIDENCE: The hold branch sets _last_move_tier = 0 (draft line 393), which is also the constructor default (agents.py:1623). The movement reason stays 'exiting_with_victim' (agents.py:1848-1853), and the ff_steps row changes only in position (wf1_results.txt:1693). The verification record recommended a distinct 'exiting_hold' category (wf1_results.txt:1695); the draft omits it. M1-M9 contain no hold metric. The bound: a hold needs every in-grid neighbour burning at once, and a cell burns at most FUEL_UPPER_LIMIT 10 / BURNING_RATE 1 = 10 fire ticks x FIRE_SPREAD_SPEED 3 = 30 steps (common_fixed_variables.py:22-23 and 30; agents.py:105-112). The draft says only 'the first step any neighbour stops burning, it moves on' (lines 400-401).
FIX(reviewer): Add metric M-HOLD, computed exactly from ff_steps: a held step is exiting, alive, pos equal to the previous row's, and not a completion. For each hold record the arm, set, instrument, start, length and end cause: moved on / died in custody (a firefighter_fire_casualty unassign of an exiting unit) / isolation release / horizon. For each death in custody, compute offline from burn_intervals whether any other living unit had a non-burning path to the victim's cell during the hold. That is the evidence that no help was withheld. Pre-register: 0 holds at HOLD 0, every hold at most 30 steps. Optionally emit an 'exiting_hold' movement-reason category at HOLD != 0; it is label-only and leaves ff_steps unchanged.
VERIFIER: The core of the finding holds up. Two of its claims are overstated, and one part of its fix is vacuous.

What checks out:
- None of M1-M9 counts holds (draft 750-803).
- M3 counts only matched clC drops, so it misses any hold on a trajectory that diverged. M2 does give end causes for carries that broke, but a hold that ends by moving on and then completing is never identified as a hold.
- A hold is not exported as its own field:
  - The hold branch sets _last_move_tier = 0 (draft line 393), which is also the constructor default (agents.py:1623).
  - The carrying branch still records "exiting_with_victim" (agents.py:1848-1853).
  - For firefighters the transition key is only the notable category "exiting" (movement_explainability.py:28-46), so a hold adds no transition-log entry.
  - Neither movement_reason nor _movement_transition_log is written to the harness JSON; ff_steps is (pos, status, assigned, exiting, dead) (_ffr_harness.py:510-520, 680-737).
- The draft never states a bound on how long a hold lasts. Line 457 even says a hold "can last long enough to reach 30".
- The bound is real:
  - A hold needs every in-grid 4-neighbour burning; otherwise `scored` is non-empty and the unit always moves (agents.py:2393-2465).
  - Fuel is randint(7,10) and drops by 1 per fire tick. Fire ticks every FIRE_SPREAD_SPEED = 3 steps, and a cell turns burnt at fuel 0 (agents.py:23, 95-123; common_fixed_variables.py:22-23, 30).
  - Fuel is never raised, and extinguish only shortens a burn (agents.py:137-152). The constants are star-imported (agents.py:10), so --set cannot change them.
  - So a cell burns at most 3 x fuel <= 30 consecutive steps, and each hold lasts at most 30 advances.
- A stronger problem the finding does not name (draft 824-825): HOLD's "inert" test says "M3 conversions > 0 OR M2 drops -> 0". At HOLD 1 the second half is automatically true, because agents.py:2411 is the only place a route_blocked can be raised for an exiting unit. So the test can never flag the fix as inert.
- Second confound, in M1/G2: a converted drop has no completion in clC, so it is not a leg there. In clH/clA it becomes a new completed leg whose length includes the hold, and it is likely stalled. clA's G2 check ("not higher in any single set") can then fail because a victim was saved.

What is overstated or wrong:
- "Never states how a hold can end": the draft does give moving on (400-401), death (453-455) and isolation (456-457). What it lacks is the horizon ending, a complete list of endings, and the bound.
- The proposed per-death check ("did any other living unit have a non-burning path to the victim's cell during the hold") always comes out empty. _safe_path_reachable_cells is a 4-connected search (wildfire_model.py:4964-4984), and during a hold all 4 neighbours of the carrier's cell are burning. That is the draft's own 3.3(a) argument, not new evidence.
- The definition "exiting, alive, same pos" misses a carrier that dies on its first held step. The casualty sweep clears exiting in the same step (wildfire_model.py:5314-5336), so that row shows dead and not exiting, with no held row.
- The optional 'exiting_hold' label would not reach Part 3's data (it is not exported). It is dashboard-only. It also adds transition/timeline entries at HOLD != 0 unless it is mapped into notable_firefighter_movement_category (movement_explainability.py:30).
- "0 holds at HOLD 0" is not guaranteed by the code's structure. A carrier already labelled route_blocked (the second-raise case) stays in place with its victim at HOLD 0 as well; the record has 0 such cases (wf1_results.txt:1629-1633).

Severity medium is fair: without these changes the report could call HOLD effective, or clA failed, for the wrong reason.
FIX(verified): 1. In section 3.2, state the bound and the full list of endings:
   - A hold needs every in-grid 4-neighbour burning (agents.py:2393-2413).
   - A cell burns at most 3 x fuel <= 30 consecutive steps: fuel randint(7,10), minus 1 per tick, a tick every 3 steps. Extinguish only shortens a burn, and the constants are star-imported, so --set cannot change them.
   - So each hold lasts at most 30 advances.
   - A hold ends in one of four ways: moved on (the first step any neighbour is not burning), died in custody, isolation release (SERVED 0 arms only), or horizon.

2. Add M-HOLD to section 9.3. Compute it exactly from ff_steps, unassigns and completions, with no new instrument:
   - A held advance at step t: the unit's step t-1 row is exiting and alive at p, its step t row is exiting and alive at p, and it has no completion at t.
   - Also count a hold that ends at once: step t-1 row exiting and alive at p, and a firefighter_fire_casualty unassign of that unit at step t. Its row shows dead and not exiting because the sweep clears exiting (wildfire_model.py:5334-5336). Record it as length 0 or 1, cause died.
   - For each hold record: arm, set, instrument, tuple, unit, victim, start step, length, end cause.
   - Pre-register:
     - every hold is at most 30 advances;
     - 0 holds at HOLD 0; any hold there can only come from the second-raise path (0 recorded) and is reported, not waived.
   - Split "moved on" by whether the neighbour that cleared was extinguished by a unit (firefight_log) or stopped burning on its own. This measures the draft's 3.3(c) claim that firefighting opens enclosures.
   - Do NOT add the "non-burning path to the victim's cell" check. It always comes out empty because the search is 4-connected (wildfire_model.py:4964-4984); state it once as a structural fact.

3. Section 9.4, HOLD's inert rule: replace "M2 drops -> 0 with no conversion" (automatically true at HOLD 1) with "M-HOLD count > 0 in the arm". Keep "M3 conversions > 0" as the effect measure.

4. M1/G2: record held steps per leg, and report a hold-adjusted stall (leg length minus held steps > d + 5) next to the primary count.
   - In clH and clA, a leg that is a broken carry in clC (a converted drop) is listed separately.
   - It does not count as a new stall against G2's "not higher in any single set".

5. The 'exiting_hold' category is optional and dashboard-only: neither harness exports movement_reason or _movement_transition_log. If it is added, map it in notable_firefighter_movement_category, or accept the new transition and timeline entries at HOLD != 0.

- The verification record lists these same slow seed-42 tests under the HOLD fix. wf1_results.txt:1647-1653, under "Hold-custody fix at agents.py:2411-2413", cites test_firefighter_fire_avoidance.py:207-259 and test_unresolved_victim_coverage.py:300-315 and says "They fail only if a carry or hold is still going at step 300."
- The mechanism is real and specific to HOLD. At HOLD 0 a dropped victim is relabelled "confirmed" and in every recorded case died 0-11 steps later, which is terminal. At HOLD 1 it stays "assigned", which is in the test's silent set {candidate, assigned, unknown} (lines 241-256), until the carrier completes, dies, or is escaped by isolation 30 steps later. With SERVED ON, that last way out is gone as well.
- Section 7's own parenthetical, "a carry still held at 300", describes HOLD's behaviour.

Refinements to the reviewer's fix:
(a) The fix is too narrow. MODE 1 and MODE 2 also change the seed-42 trajectory: completion timing, dispatch and the positions of units freed earlier. So every slow full-run test can move under ANY flipped fix. That includes test_remaining_victim_coverage_after_two_rescues (test_unresolved_victim_coverage.py:282-300), whose UAV-position and unresolved-count asserts depend on the trajectory. HOLD and SERVED are only the fixes with a DIRECT route to a non-terminal victim at step 300.
(b) The fix is also more than needed. Running the suite for every fix plus the combination every time costs up to 4 x 36-70 min. The gate only has to hold at the defaults that actually ship. Per-fix runs are needed only to attribute a failure.

Severity medium is correct. As written, the design could ship a fix that breaks a passing slow test without any pre-registered stop, which misses the brief's "no NEW test failures" gate.

VERIFIER: I tried to refute the finding and could not. The core claim holds; one part of its reasoning overstates the case.

The free reference exists and is valid for 6160438 at defaults.
- outputs/_ug_rbD_queue.txt holds four lines of the form `rb|ugGD{a,b,c,s}|E:/Projects/SAS|...|240|`. The last field (extra args) is empty, so no --set was passed.
- outputs/_ug_rbD_wave.log:1-3 shows the pool started at head=ba58491 with dirty_tracked=0, on Python 3.10.11 with mesa 1.2.1, and prints the SHIPPED defaults.
- `git diff --stat ba58491 6160438 -- . ':(exclude)outputs'` is empty.
- outputs/_rblatch_campaign2.py last changed at 68c75a9, which is an ancestor of ba58491. The shards are committed in 03ebb56, which is in 6160438.
- The verification record agrees: wf1_results.txt:509 calls ugGD "today's source".
- The recorded shard content is deterministic apart from evals[*].wall_s. It holds stats, exact, exact_fires, exact_recoveries, fires, recoveries, assigns, latched, deaths and params. The params dict carries no extra keys.

The omission is real.
- Draft 9.1 (lines 714-725) identity-checks only the stock and CRN harness paths.
- Draft 9.6 runs the rbgate shards only in step 3.
- Draft M8 (788-789) treats clGC as "re-measured at this commit". It never compares clGC with ugGD.
- There is precedent for exactly this check. The ungated round's G5 did it: _ug_rbgate.py:124-127 checks "ugGC == sfRBG (same source)", and _ug_rbgate.txt:205 records it at 18/18.

The route_blocked path is a separate instrument, so the C13 identity check does not cover it:
- it runs 4-5 seeds in one process, and its globals (STATS, EXACT, _blocked_ever, _recovered_ever) accumulate across those seeds;
- it enters through serve_dashboard._build_evaluation;
- it calls model.get_dashboard_state() every step until the run is terminal (not every step, as the finding says);
- it monkeypatches _mark_route_blocked, _revalidate_route_blocked_firefighters and apply_physical_rescue_command.

A module-level state leak across seeds, or drift on this path, cannot show up in one-seed-per-process harness runs.

Where the finding overstates: G5 compares each arm with ihrest (draft 786-788). It does not compare with clGC, so the arms' G5 does not literally "rest on" clGC. The real value of the check is different. Without it, drift on the route_blocked path would silently move every arm's G5 against a stale recorded reference. Worse, the "re-measured" clGC would absorb that drift as if it were a new baseline, which is the baseline-moved failure mode. There is a second benefit: ugGD's recorded regression set against ihrest is {east/707, south/101, south/404}, only 3 of the known four (ungated_report.txt:466). A passing identity check therefore pins the control's regression set at this commit.

Severity: medium is defensible, since this is a zero-cost check whose absence could mislead G5 attribution. The brief itself demands identity only on the canonical 13, so this is hardening beyond the brief, not a missed requirement.

VERIFIER: I could not refute the finding. Every part of it holds against the code.

1. The pool picks one harness for all its lines. outputs/_ug_pool.sh:27 sets `HARNESS=${HARNESS:-outputs/_ffr_harness.py}`, and :183 runs `$PY "$HARNESS" --repo ... $extra` for every ff line. The queue fields are kind|tag|repo|wind|roles|seeds|steps|extra (:12, :82, :179), and none of them names a harness. The draft (686-689) asks for a single output, `_cl_queue.py -> _cl_queue.txt`, for one copy of _ug_pool.sh, yet wants stock lines through _ffr_harness.py (9.3, 751) and CRN lines through _fm2_probe_harness.py. One pool invocation cannot do both. The ug precedent split them: _ug_queue.py:5 says "_ug_crn_queue.txt the CRN arms (run with HARNESS=outputs/_fm2_probe_harness.py)", and _ug_crn_wave.log shows "wave=crn ... queue=outputs/_ug_crn_queue.txt". ungated_part1.txt:699 says the same.

2. The dead input is silent. The plain harness puts each --set into `extra` (_ffr_harness.py:156-162) and applies it with apply_scenario_config (src_extension/adaptation/local_adaptation_generator.py:270-273). That function is a bare setattr on cfv and wf with no unknown-key check. Nothing in agents.py, wildfire_model.py or common_fixed_variables.py reads FM2P*; grep finds 0 hits. Only the probe's wrapper reads the key (_fm2_probe_harness.py:115) and writes the "fm2p" block (:204-211). A CRN line run through the plain harness is therefore a stock run whose extra_params still say {'FM2P_CRN': 1}.

3. The validators do not catch it. _dcd4_validate.check (:82-120) compares only tag, wind, roles, seed, steps, extra_params, uav_actions, eval, the stdout sha and lines, and the .out seed line. _dcd4rb_validate imports that check unchanged. grep for fm2p in both files finds 0 hits. The mislabelled run matches its queue line on extra_params, so it validates clean. Draft 694 does make _cl_analyze refuse a run whose "fm2p state" is wrong, so the error is caught, but only after the runs are spent.

Two refinements:
(a) The cost may be smaller than the finding says. The finding says 511 CRN runs. If the 9.6 staging is followed and the screen is analysed before step 3, the error shows up after the 91 C13 CRN runs. It is still a lost wave, so medium stands.
(b) The validator hole exists even with the right harness. The probe adds fm2p only after runpy returns. By then the harness has already written the JSON, the stdout.txt and the final "seed=" line (_ffr_harness.py:839, :844). A probe killed in that window leaves a JSON with no fm2p that the current check calls VALID. The pool's pre-launch and orphan ADOPTED paths (_ug_pool.sh:276-282, :310) would then accept a stock run as CRN. A leftover `<out>.fm2p.tmp` (probe :209) is not checked either.

Also relevant: each pool's orphan snapshot matches only its own harness (-HarnessLike "*${HARNESS##*/}*", :119), and the lock, state and attempts files are keyed by STEM (:47-50). The two queues must therefore be tag-disjoint. The pools should also run one after the other, as the ug CRN wave did: only _ug_state.txt exists, so it reused STEM=outputs/_ug. Otherwise they need distinct STEM and LOG values and a split MAXPAR, because two concurrent pools double the parallelism budget.
FIX(verified): Change section 8.3 as follows:

(1) _cl_queue.py writes two queues, following the ug precedent:
- _cl_queue.txt holds the stock ff lines and the rb lines. Its pool runs with the default HARNESS=outputs/_ffr_harness.py.
- _cl_crn_queue.txt holds only the lines carrying `--set FM2P_CRN=1`. Its pool runs with HARNESS=outputs/_fm2_probe_harness.py.
- Each pool gets its own WAVE and LOG. Run them one after the other, sharing STEM as ug did. If they must overlap, give each its own STEM and split MAXPAR, because the lock, state and attempts files are keyed by STEM.
- _cl_queue.py asserts at write time that every line in _cl_crn_queue.txt carries FM2P_CRN=1 and that no line in _cl_queue.txt carries any FM2P_* key.
- Section 9.5/9.6 names both launch command lines with their HARNESS.

(2) Point the pool copy's VAL at a copied validator, _cl_validate.py, that wraps _dcd4rb_validate. Add one rule for ff lines:
- Let want = {k: v for k, v in sets.items() if k.startswith('FM2P_')}.
- If want is non-empty, require d.get('fm2p', {}).get('config') == want, with the value types equal. This is needed because True == 1.
- If want is empty, require 'fm2p' not in d.
- Treat a leftover `<json>.fm2p.tmp` as INVALID, the same as `.json.tmp`.

A mismatch is then INVALID at validate time. That covers the wrong-harness case and the second case where the probe is killed after the harness wrote its files but before the fm2p block was written. Without the rule, that run passes the existing check and could be ADOPTED as a CRN run. Keep the fm2p check in _cl_analyze as a second line of defence.

Nowhere does the draft name a restore fixture. tests/ has no conftest.py, so nothing restores these names globally.

2. The project precedent is consistent. Every feature-switch test module restores its config:
   - test_firefighter_fire_mechanic.py:58-72 (over _SWITCHES + SYSTEM_RANDOM on (cfv, wf); deletes absent names; restores agents.random)
   - test_firefighter_rescue_absence.py:34-48
   - test_victim_fire_flight.py:37-48
   - test_route_block_stale_clear.py:52-56, whose docstring reads "Never leak a monkeypatched switch into another module's tests"
   Several older modules call apply_scenario_config with no restore and already leak their own keys: dashboard_panel/state/explanations, executor_routing, fire_avoidance, unresolved_victim_coverage, scenario_matrix. The suite is not leak-proof, so a new leak would go unnoticed.

3. The exposure is concrete. The seed-42 300-step terminal tests call apply_scenario_config without any FF_EXIT_LEG_* key, so they inherit whatever the last new test left:
   - test_firefighter_fire_avoidance.py:205-225
   - test_unresolved_victim_coverage.py:32-45 and 305-316
   - test_y_sweep_commit.py:111-117
   Any plausible name for the new module (test_carry_leg, test_exit_leg, test_firefighter_exit_leg) sorts before all of them and before test_wind_aware_trajectory_regression. The draft itself (section 7, lines 642-645) says SERVED ON can change those terminal tests. A leaked SERVED=1, MODE=2 or HOLD=1 would therefore make G6's "default-OFF" suite actually run the features in them. That could add a spurious new failure, costing an investigation, or change or mask the failing-name set that G6 compares against the 8.

The fix needs one refinement. A canary that runs "last or as a session-finish check" cannot be done as stated: pytest has no run-last guarantee, and a sessionfinish hook needs a new tests/conftest.py that every module would collect under. Only the new tests write these names, so a per-test check inside the restore fixture covers the whole risk without depending on test order. Compare against the values captured at setup, not hard-coded zeros, so the check survives the flip commit. Severity medium is fair: the risk is a mis-measured G6 gate, not a wrong design.

CLAIM: A successful HOLD or SERVED adds long completed legs to M1. A converted drop, or a carry no longer released at pickup+29, completes late and is counted as stalled. VERIFIER: The core claim holds against the code, the recorded data and the draft. Three of the reviewer's details are wrong, but none of them weakens it.

1. A successful HOLD or SERVED adds stalled legs to M1-primary, by construction.
- M1 counts only completed legs. Stalled means leg > d+5 (_xs_census.py:5-8, 84, 163-164; draft 9.3 M1).
- Every convertible drop in the record was already a long carry when it was dropped. Pickup, d and the hold-alone completion come from wf1_results.txt:1226-1270 and the census broken-leg list:
  - uhD/uhY east/half/1291443119: pickup 84 at (41,33), d=8, completes at 163. Leg 79 > 13.
  - fmDRY east/half/101: pickup 100 at (35,14), d=14, completes at 150. Leg 50 > 19.
  - f2cEFS east/half/404: pickup 104 at (40,10), d=9, completes at 146 or 152. Leg 42-48 > 14.
- For SERVED the result is exact on 50x50 (common_fixed_variables.py:20-21):
  - d = min(x, 49-x, y, 49-y) <= 24, so d+5 <= 29.
  - The streak that SERVED suppresses marks at 30 (rescue_planner.py:499-510). In 30 of 32 records the marking came at pickup+29.
  - So any carry that SERVED keeps and that later completes is at least 30 steps long, which is a stall. The f2cDRY east/def/101 case is an example: 3 cells from the exit at 177, with a leg of about 33 against d+5 = 21.

2. The draft contradicts itself.
- 9.4 (lines 814-815) says HOLD and SERVED "are not expected to move stalls".
- Its own inert rule (lines 822-825) requires HOLD to show M3 conversions > 0. Every conversion adds an M1 stall, so a non-inert HOLD raises stalls, and so does a non-inert SERVED.
- The report would show the HOLD and SERVED arms' stall counts rising, with a rationale that says the opposite.

3. The combination is charged for the other fixes' successes.
- G2 applies to "MODE arms". clA contains MODE 2.
- The gate runs "again for the combination", and shipping requires the combination to pass G1-G6 (line 826).
- So HOLD and SERVED conversions inside clA count against "not higher in any single set".
- Whether this bites on recorded data is uncertain. In uhD the carrier sat on the boundary from 107 to 142, so mode 1 alone completes it long before the drop at 148. Mode 2 most likely pre-empts the one convertible drop in the shipped configuration.
- The problem is still structural, and C13 makes it brittle. In the shipped configuration C13 has 0 stalls in 37 completed legs (ugD, 240 steps; my script c13_stalls.py). A single converted leg in clA on C13 fails the per-set test.

4. The brief's gate is literal and applies per fix: "stalled exit legs FALL" plus "If one fix passes and another does not...". HOLD and SERVED cannot meet it when they work. The draft exempts them, but lists that departure nowhere in D-1..D-6.

Corrections to the finding:
- The draft does state the exemption (lines 814-815). It is not silent. What it gets wrong is the rationale, and it never asks the maintainer.
- The uhD drop was at 148, not 150. 150 is when the victim died.
- C13 in the shipped configuration carries 0 stalls, not "about 1-2". That makes the per-set flip more likely, not less.

5. The proposed fix only half-works for clA.
- Matching to clC by (tuple, unit, victim, pickup step and cell) finds conversions on each run's first carry.
- Mode 2 makes later pickups diverge from clC. A conversion or custody-save on a later carry would have no matched clC carry and would still be counted.
- Conversions therefore also need to be identified from the arm's own record. The harness does not record the tier label (no "tier" in _ffr_harness.py), so this has to be derived from ff_steps and burn_intervals.

No simulation was run. The quarantine directory was not touched: the only outputs/ access was named files plus one non-recursive shell glob, `ls _ffr_ugD_*.json`.

(2) In M1, add a column for legs saved from a break, and report each such leg's length and d. A completed leg in the column is one that meets any of these three tests:
- (a) Its matched clC carry (same tuple, instrument, unit, victim, pickup step and cell) ended broken: a route_blocked drop, an isolation release or the horizon. This is the M3 match.
- (b) In an arm with HOLD on, the leg contains a held advance: the unit is exiting, its cell is unchanged across the advance, and every in-grid 4-neighbour is burning per burn_intervals.
- (c) In an arm with SERVED on, the leg is 30 or more steps long, and at pickup+29 no other live, on-grid, non-exiting unit existed, per ff_steps.
Tests (b) and (c) are needed because mode 2 makes carries after the first diverge from clC, so (a) alone misses conversions on those later carries in clA.

CLAIM: The in-fire half of M7 is always 0 by construction, because a firefighter on a burning cell is dead before the harness records. Draft M7: 'Mode 2 is predicted to cut the adjacent count (replay 123 -> 12 including its smoke reconstruction)'.
FIX(reviewer): Define M7's in-fire term as carrier deaths on the carrying leg (the dead row whose previous row was exiting), plus deaths of carried victims. Report end-of-step adjacency under its real name. VERIFIER: WHAT HOLDS: the in-fire half of M7 is always 0 by construction.
- Fire agents update inside self.schedule.step() (wildfire_model.py:5844). The casualty sweep runs after that, in the post-move cycle (wildfire_model.py:5845, which calls adaptation_manager.py:182 `_check_fire_casualties`).
- The sweep builds fire_cells from Fire.is_burning(). That function returns .burning (agents.py:38-39), the same field the harness reads.
- Any live unit on one of those cells is set dead=True, status "dead", exiting=False (wildfire_model.py:5302-5336).
- The harness records ff_steps and burn_intervals after model.step() returns (_ffr_harness.py:484-520). Between the sweep and the record, nothing writes fire.
- Checked on the recorded data (767 files: _ffr_fm*/f2c*/uh*/ug*, read-only script): 0 of 18,910 rows that were exiting and alive sat on a burning cell. All 18 death rows that follow an exiting row have exiting=False and a burning cell.
- So the draft's "in fire (own cell burning)" column will print 0 in every arm. Carriers do die in fire, so that 0 is misleading, and the brief's "steps ... in fire" goes unmeasured.

WHAT IS REFUTED: the claim that M7's adjacency is not what the replay's 123 -> 12 measured.
- The replay counts env.adj(pos, k) after the move (fs_counterfactual.py:93). Here k is the post-step-k board: burn_intervals are the post-step observation (fs_env.py:3). That is exactly M7's definition: the end-of-step cell against end-of-step fire.
- The two timings also coincide. In mesa 1.2.1, SimultaneousActivation advances agents in the order they were added. Fire agents are added at construction (new_fire_agent, wildfire_model.py:2358-2365), before the firefighter markers (905/971). By the time a carrier advances, every Fire.advance of that step has already run, so the board it decides on is the post-step board (fs_legs.py:4-5 says this too).
- Empirical check: I recomputed adjacency from the recorded ff_steps positions against burn_intervals, over the replay's own legs and window (s0+1..s1-1):
  - census: 123, equal to the replay's base 123;
  - uhD: 16 = 16;
  - ugKD: 9 = 9;
  - on the legs whose base replay matches the recording (198/100/96), 0 per-leg mismatches.
- Smoke reconstruction only changes which cells the counterfactual trajectory visits; it does not change the count's definition. The CL_OBS probe is not needed, and the comparison should not be withdrawn.
- There is a small difference in population. The replay counts completed legs only, steps s0+1..s1-1. M7 counts every carrying row, including broken legs. On the same census files M7's definition gives 129, not 123.

WHAT THE REVIEWER MISSED: real in-fire exposure exists and can be computed from existing records.
- It is a carrying step whose starting cell (the unit's cell at the end of step k-1) is burning on the step-k board. This is the cell the carrier stood on when it decided, and it cannot be seen at the end of the step.
- In the 767 files: 197 such steps. 179 carriers stepped out and survived; the other 18 are the deaths.

[measure] P3-08 medium sec 9.3 M4 VERDICT CONFIRMED
CLAIM: The 'in custody at that step' test depends on which recorded row is read. At the marking step the escape has already unassigned the carrier before the harness records, so a test on the after-step row gives 0 in clC too, and 'SERVED -> M4 -> 0' passes without testing anything. Reading the previous row instead misclassifies a carry dropped earlier in the same step.
EVIDENCE: wildfire_model.py:5844-5846: schedule.step(), post-move cycle, then _update_unreachable_victims(). wildfire_model.py:5108-5119: the escape issues 'unassign' with reason=cause for the unit _find_active_firefighter_for_victim returns. _ffr_harness.py:372-377 records it in unassigns with step = evaluation_timesteps_counter. ff_steps index i is the state after step i+1.
FIX(reviewer): Define a custody marking as an unassigns row {step s, ff, vid, reason 'geographically_isolated'} whose ff was exiting in ff_steps[s-2] and bound to vid in ff_bind_steps[s-2]. VERIFIER: The finding holds on the code, on the recorded data and on the draft text. Its fix is right in substance but needs two refinements.

Code (checked at 6160438):
- wildfire_model.py:5815 increments evaluation_timesteps_counter before schedule.step() at :5844. After that come the post-move cycle (:5845) and _update_unreachable_victims (:5846).
- The escape at :5106-5119 unassigns the unit that _find_active_firefighter_for_victim returns (:3728-3748, which skips route_blocked units). The unassign at :4715-4719 clears exiting, rescued_victim and exit_target.
- The harness stamps unassigns with the counter (_ffr_harness.py:366-377, _step_of at :176-177). It appends ff_steps and ff_bind_steps only after model.step() returns (:484, :520, :535). So a marking stamped s shows the carrier already released in ff_steps[s-1], and still in custody only in ff_steps[s-2] and ff_bind_steps[s-2].

Draft: section 9.3 M4 says only "in custody at that step (from unreachable_escape_log + ff_bind_steps + ff_steps)" and never names the row. The escape-log rows carry no ff id (:5126-5134). M7 in the same section reads ff_steps "at the end of the step". The project convention (wf1_results.txt:276) is that an event stamped s is in ff_steps[s-1]. So the natural reading is the after-step row.

Recorded data: my script review/verify_p3_08/m4_rows.py listed outputs/ non-recursively and skipped the quarantine by name. It covered 2524 _ffr_*.json files and 160 geographically_isolated escape entries.
- 31 entries have the victim bound to an exiting, live, on-grid unit in row s-2. All 31 have a matching isolated unassign by that unit.
- 0 of the 160 show custody in row s-1.
- f2cDRY east/def/101: row 175 shows ff_unit_1 exiting and bound to victim_0; row 176 shows it not exiting and bound to nothing.

So an after-step-row M4 is 0 in clC by construction. "SERVED -> M4 -> 0" would then pass without testing anything.

The second claim, that the before-step row alone would count a carry dropped earlier in the same step, is correct in principle. At HOLD 0 a route_blocked drop inside step s happens before the escape. It has 0 recorded instances, and requiring the isolated unassign by the same unit excludes it: after the drop, the escape pairs with a replacement or with nobody.

Refinement 1: the proposed separate count of "escape-log entries with no matching unassign" is wrong as literally written. 129 of the 160 recorded markings have no unassign at all: all units dead, co-marked victims, off-grid hand-over. That bin would swamp the real route_blocked no-pair custody case. It must be restricted to victims that were in custody at s-2.

Refinement 2: the mechanism check must require clC M4 > 0. Otherwise "-> 0" is still vacuous on seed sets where the situation never arises.

The 9-case validation should expect 31 records, not 32: one of the 32 is in a vmon file with no ff_bind_steps. Medium severity is appropriate: M4 is report-only, but it decides whether SERVED gets called working or inert.
FIX(verified): Replace the M4 text in section 9.3 with an explicit row-indexed definition:

"M4 ISOLATION MARKINGS. Row convention: an event stamped s happens during model step s. ff_steps[s-1] and ff_bind_steps[s-1] are the state AFTER step s, which is after the escape. The state at the START of step s is row s-2.

A CUSTODY MARKING is an unreachable_escape_log entry {step s, victim v, cause geographically_isolated} for which some unit F:
(a) is exiting, not dead and on grid in ff_steps[s-2], and is bound to v in ff_bind_steps[s-2];
(b) has no unassigns row at step s for (F, v) with a reason other than geographically_isolated, and no completion at step s (so it was not dropped or completed earlier in the same step).
Split custody markings into:
  M4a: there is an unassigns row {s, F, v, reason geographically_isolated} (the normal escape release);
  M4b: there is no unassign for (F, v) at s (the carrier was labelled route_blocked and the escape found no pair).
Report separately the co-marked other victims (escape entries at step s that are not custody markings), which are rung 2's population. Never read row s-1 for custody: the escape has already cleared it."

Before any wave, the analyzer must reproduce the recorded census: 31 records with ff_bind_steps (32 including the vmon3 file, which has no ff_bind_steps), 9 distinct cases, f2cDRY east/def/101 victim_0 @177 by ff_unit_1, 0 in M4b. It must STOP on any mismatch.

In 9.4, replace "SERVED -> M4 custody markings -> 0" with: "SERVED -> clC M4a+M4b > 0 and arm M4a+M4b = 0 on the same set/instrument. If clC's custody markings are 0 on a set, report SERVED as untested there (no opportunity), not as passing."
