[measure] P3-10 medium sec 8.3 (_cl_queue / pool) VERDICT CONFIRMED
CLAIM: A single queue that mixes stock and CRN lines will not run correctly on a copy of _ug_pool.sh. HARNESS is global to the pool, so a 'CRN' line runs the stock harness with FM2P_CRN=1 as a --set that does nothing. The pool validator accepts it because extra_params match, and the error surfaces only at analysis, after the wave.
EVIDENCE: _ug_pool.sh:27 'HARNESS=${HARNESS:-outputs/_ffr_harness.py}', :183 '$PY "$HARNESS" --repo ...'. The queue line is kind|tag|repo|wind|roles|seeds|steps|extra with no harness field. The live watcher filters on -HarnessLike "*${HARNESS##*/}*" (_ug_pool.sh:119). The ungated round ran a separate CRN pool (survey section 7).
FIX(reviewer): Use two queues and two pools (stock; CRN with HARNESS=outputs/_fm2_probe_harness.py), each with its own TAGPFX/STEM. Or add a 'crn' kind that selects the probe. Make the validator require an fm2p block with config {'FM2P_CRN': 1} on CRN lines and its absence on stock lines.
VERIFIER: The finding holds against the code, the recorded precedent and the draft text.

1. The draft specifies one queue with no way to pick a harness per line. Section 8.3 (draft lines 685-689) names a single file, _cl_queue.txt, "for a copy of _ug_pool.sh (kind|tag|repo|wind|roles|seeds|steps|extra)". That one file holds the ff lines, "CRN lines through _fm2_probe_harness.py with FM2P_CRN=1" and the rb lines. The queue line has no harness field. The pool accepts only kinds ff and rb (_ug_pool.sh:83-87, where any other kind is "BAD QUEUE LINE", exit 2), and the validator does the same (_dcd4rb_validate.py:93-94, SystemExit on an unknown kind). HARNESS is set once per pool (_ug_pool.sh:27, `HARNESS=${HARNESS:-outputs/_ffr_harness.py}`), and every ff line runs through it (:183). The draft never says how a CRN line would reach the probe.

2. With the stock harness, a CRN line fails silently:
   - _ffr_harness.py:156-162 puts `--set FM2P_CRN=1` into params/extra_params, and apply_scenario_config (local_adaptation_generator.py:270-273) only calls setattr.
   - Nothing in agents.py or wildfire_model.py reads FM2P_* (0 Grep hits in each), so the run uses stock fire.
   - The pool's ff check (_dcd4_validate.py check) compares extra_params with the queue's sets, so the run matches. It never looks at an fm2p block, so the run is VALID and counts as done.

3. The error shows up only after the wave. The draft's _cl_analyze.py refuses runs by "fm2p state" (8.3), as _ug_analyze.load does at :122-126 ("CRN arm without an fm2p block"). That check runs after the wave. No wrong result would be published, but the whole CRN half (511 runs, about 6 h by the draft's own section 9.5) would be spent for nothing. That fits the medium severity.

4. The precedent agrees. The ungated round split the work: _ug_queue.py:4-5 writes _ug_crn_queue.txt "(run with HARNESS=outputs/_fm2_probe_harness.py)". _ug_crn_wave.log shows a separate pool: wave=crn, its own queue, STEM _ugk (state file _ugk_state.txt), maxpar 5. The survey records that it ran alongside the stock pool. The POOL START line does not log HARNESS, so even there the wave log does not prove which harness ran.

5. On the reviewer's fix:
   - Running two queues and two pools is correct, and it is the precedent.
   - A new 'crn' kind is the more invasive option. It needs changes to the pool's launch/OUT_OF logic and to _dcd4rb_validate.read_queue. I do not recommend it.
   - Running the whole mixed queue through the probe would work in principle, because the probe passes through when no FM2P key is set (_fm2_probe_harness.py:10-13). But it contradicts the draft's own definition of the STOCK instrument as _ffr_harness.py (9.3).
   - A router harness in the style of _xs_probe.py:342-352 (it dispatches on FM2P_CRN in argv) would also work, but it adds an instrument layer.
   - The validator part of the fix is right, but it must not edit the shared _dcd4rb_validate.py. The pool copy should point its VAL at a new wrapper instead.

No command touched the quarantine. The only listing was a non-recursive `ls outputs/` piped to grep, and Grep ran only on agents.py and wildfire_model.py.
FIX(verified): Rewrite section 8.3 as follows:

(a) Write two queues, as the ungated round did. _cl_queue.py writes:
   - _cl_queue.txt: the stock ff lines plus the rb gate lines.
   - _cl_crn_queue.txt: the CRN ff lines only, each carrying `--set FM2P_CRN=1`.
   Nothing ever carries FM2P_CRN=0.

(b) Run two launches of the one pool copy.
   - Stock pool: default HARNESS.
   - CRN pool: HARNESS=outputs/_fm2_probe_harness.py.
   - Each pool gets its own QUEUE, STEM, LOG and WAVE, for example STEM=outputs/_clk and LOG=outputs/_cl_crn_wave.log, following the _ugk / _ug_crn_wave.log precedent. The lock, state, attempts and quarantine files must not collide.
   - Pick a TAGPFX that matches only that pool's tags. _dcd4rb_live.ps1 filters on it together with HarnessLike.

(c) Add a start-up guard to the pool copy, before the validator pass. Refuse to start (exit 2) if any ff line's extra contains `FM2P_` while `${HARNESS##*/}` is not `_fm2_probe_harness.py`, or if any ff line lacks it while HARNESS is the probe. Also add `harness=$HARNESS` to the POOL START log line for provenance, since today it is not logged.

(d) Point the copy's VAL (_ug_pool.sh:52) at a new outputs/_cl_validate.py. Leave the shared _dcd4rb_validate.py unedited. The new file imports read_queue, check and quarantine from _dcd4rb_validate. For each ff run, after the existing check returns VALID:
   - if FM2P_CRN is in the run's sets, require `d.get('fm2p', {}).get('config') == {'FM2P_CRN': 1}`;
   - otherwise require `'fm2p' not in d`.
   With this, a wrong-harness run is INVALID at its first judge(), or refused at adoption, instead of passing the pool and being caught only by _cl_analyze after the wave.

(e) Keep _cl_analyze.py's provenance check as the final backstop.

[measure] P3-12 medium sec 9.4 G3 (and G2 'both instruments') VERDICT CONFIRMED
CLAIM: The brief asks 'rescued does not decrease at 360 on any seed set' (3 comparisons); the draft makes it 6 by requiring it in both instruments. It gives no rule for stock and CRN disagreeing. At the per-set re-roll noise already recorded, a neutral or mildly positive MODE 2 fails at least one of the six comparisons with high probability.
EVIDENCE: Recorded per-set rescued differences (my count): uhC->uhD 104->102 (4 down / 2 up) at 360; ugC->ugD 101->93; ugKC->ugKY 95->97. Draft 9.4 G3: 'in either instrument (6 comparisons per arm)'. Brief: 'CRN for any per-seed claim'.
FIX(reviewer): Pre-register which instrument decides each set, e.g. stock and CRN both reported and CRN deciding when they disagree, with every flipped victim listed per seed. Or keep both, but state the expected false-fail rate and route a single-instrument loss to the maintainer with its per-victim attribution instead of an automatic FAIL.
VERIFIER: The finding holds against the draft text, the recorded runs and the project's own precedent. Its cited evidence is imprecise, and the first of its two fix options is wrong.

WHAT THE DRAFT SAYS
- G3 (draft lines 816-817) reads: "RESCUED at step 360 not lower than clC on ANY seed set, in either instrument (6 comparisons per arm)."
- The brief says: "rescued does not decrease at 360 on any seed set". It names no instrument, and it asks for CRN only "for any per-seed claim". The draft's 3 sets x 2 instruments is its own addition.
- The draft has no rule for stock and CRN disagreeing. A search for disagree, attribut, "either instrument" and "both instrument" finds none for rescued.
- Per-case attribution exists only in G4 (line 819: "without a measured, explained cause (per-death attribution under CRN)"). G3 has no such escape.
- 9.7 (line 851) turns any G3 loss into STOP-and-don't-ship.
- There is also a contradiction: G3's 6 comparisons count C13 as a gate set. The brief does require that. But 9.6 step 2 (line 842) says C13 is "never reported as a result", and 9.2 calls it "never evidence".

EVIDENCE CHECK (my own copy of the reviewer's script: review/verify_p3_12/noise_copy.py, reading recorded _ffr_*.json only)
- The cited numbers are exact:
  - uhC->uhD: 104->102, 2 up / 4 down, at 360.
  - ugC->ugD: 101->93, 3 up / 10 down, but at 240, not 360.
  - ugKC->ugKY: 95->97, CRN.
- These are NOT re-roll noise of a neutral change. They are real configuration effects: gated vs ungated, and positioning-only.
- Better noise proxies for a positioning-scale change:
  - uhD->uhY: 1 flip in 30 (stock, 360), even though the fire differs in 28 of 30 runs.
  - ugD->ugY: 2 flips in 30.
  - ugKD->ugKY: 1 flip in 30 (CRN).
- Model: flips per set ~ Poisson(lambda), each flip +/-1 with equal chance for a neutral change (review/verify_p3_12/falsefail.py). P(one set lower) is 0.27 at lambda=1 and 0.32 at lambda=1.5.
- With stock lambda about 1.5 per 30-run set (0.65 for C13) and CRN about 1.0 (0.45 for C13), the chance a neutral fix fails at least once is:
  - 3 stock comparisons (the brief): about 0.63.
  - The draft's 6 comparisons: about 0.83.
- So "high probability" is right. Most of it comes from the brief's zero-tolerance gate; the doubling adds about 20 points.
- The missing disagreement rule is not hypothetical. The last round hit exactly this case: outputs/ungated_report.txt:43-45 has STOCK ugC 101->ugD 93 BREACH next to CRN ugKC 95->ugKD 96 PASS. The round STOPPED and the maintainer decided. That round's gate also had a tolerance, M = 3; this brief has none.
- CRN is no noise-free adjudicator here either. With the fire mechanic ON, unit moves write to the fire: ugKC->ugKD is fire-identical in 0 of 30 runs and still flips 3. So "CRN decides" is weaker than it sounds.

WHERE THE REVIEWER'S FIX NEEDS CORRECTING
- Its first option, "CRN deciding when they disagree", would automatically PASS a stock loss. The brief's literal gate can be read as the stock instrument, and the maintainer's precedent treats a single-channel stock breach as a STOP. So stock must never be auto-passed.
- Its second option (report both, state the false-fail rate, route to the maintainer with per-victim attribution) is right, with CRN losses kept as fails.

SECONDARY
G2 (line 807: "in BOTH instruments, and not higher in any single set") has the same multiplicity. It matters little for MODE 2, whose predicted stall drop is large. It matters for MODE 1, whose replay predicts only 56->54, so MODE 1's G2 is close to a coin flip. The same routing rule should apply.
FIX(verified): Replace G3 (section 9.4) with a pre-registered two-channel rule:

1. The gate, as the brief states it, is rescued at 360 not lower than clC on each of C13, U30 and N30. Both instruments are measured and reported per set, never pooled, and CRN is compared only with CRN.

2. Any comparison where the arm is lower triggers per-victim attribution for that set, in both instruments. For every flipped victim, list:
   - the seed and victim id;
   - its control fate and step, and its arm fate and step;
   - whether the fire digests match up to the victim's fate;
   - the first step where the two runs differ, and what differed;
   - whether the victim's own carry was changed by the fix (the M3 match: same tuple, unit, victim, pickup).

3. A fix automatically FAILS G3 when:
   - (a) the CRN channel is lower on any set; or
   - (b) any lost victim is attributable to the fix's own mechanism, meaning its matched carry is longer or broken in the arm on a fire identical up to that point.

4. A loss in stock only, with CRN not lower on that set and no victim attributable to the fix, is neither an automatic PASS nor an automatic FAIL. The fix is held and goes to the maintainer as a STOP item with the attribution, as in the ungated round (outputs/ungated_report.txt:39-46). Add this to 9.7 and as decision D-7.

5. In D-7, state the expected false-fail rate before any run. At the recorded flip rates (uhD->uhY 1/30, ugD->ugY 2/30, ugKD->ugKY 1/30), a rescue-neutral fix fails at least one of the brief's 3 stock comparisons with probability about 0.6, and one of 6 with about 0.8. Ask the maintainer whether to accept a tolerance like the ungated round's M = 3. Do not add one unilaterally.

6. Remove the C13 contradiction. Keep C13 in G3, because the brief requires it. Reword 9.6 step 2 to say C13 is a screen for direction-of-effect claims, but its G3 comparison is a gate item.

7. Apply the same routing to G2's "not higher in any single set / both instruments" clause. A set where stalls are higher in one instrument only gets its legs listed and goes to the maintainer instead of an automatic FAIL. The pooled "strictly lower" test stays as written.

VERIFIER: The main claim holds, and the data make it stronger. Three sub-claims go too far.

CONFIRMED:
1. The draft's figure is its own extrapolation. Draft section 9.5 (lines 830-837) and D-5 (line 106) take the 516 s mean per run, measured with at most 8 runs going at once, and divide it across 12 slots: 1,035 x 516 / 12 = 12.4 h. No wave of this code at 360 steps has ever run above 8 at once.
2. The 21 h arithmetic is right. I parsed outputs/_uh_wave.log myself: 90 runs, first launch 15:34:50, last DONE 17:24:03, which is 72.8 s per run of throughput. On average 7.12 runs were going, 8 at most, with 59 throttles. 1,035 x 72.8 s = 20.9 h.
3. The reviewer missed something that makes it worse. The draft charges all 511 CRN runs at the stock 516 s. In the ug round the same tuples ran at the same concurrency (about 9.8 runs box-wide), and the CRN harness runs were slower:
   - ugKD 526 s vs ugD 438 s mean
   - ugKC 528 vs ugC 438
   - ugKY 524 vs ugY 402
   - by total time that is 1.20-1.30x; per tuple, the median ratio is 1.11-1.15 and the mean 1.27-1.28.
   With a 1.22x CRN factor, even the draft's best case is (524 x 516 + 511 x 516 x 1.22) / 12, about 13.7 h.
4. The rbgate time really is missing. D-5 only says "plus the route_blocked gate", with no time. Recorded ug shard walls sum to 12,264 s for ugGC and 7,308 s for ugGD, about 9.8 k process-seconds per arm. Over 7 arms that is about 68.5 k slot-seconds: +1.6 h if 12 run at once, +2.7 h at the horizon wave's 7.1.

OVERSTATED:
(a) 21 h is an upper bound, not the expected figure. The horizon wave was held back by commit charge: it started with only 10.1 GB free and settled at about 0.86 GB per sim (3.2 GB free with 8 running). With about 23 GB free now and MINFREE at 3.5 GB, 12 at once fits in memory. The machine has 16 cores / 32 threads, so 12 single-threaded sims do not saturate the CPU. The honest range for the draft's 1,035 runs plus rbgate is about 15 h (12 at once, no per-run slowdown, CRN overhead included) to about 26 h (the horizon wave's measured throughput). Measured data cannot say where in that range it lands: the 360/240 per-tuple ratio of 1.26-1.31 mixes step count and concurrency.
(b) "clA re-run forced whenever any single fix fails" is too broad. Under the 9.4 SHIPPING rule, a new combination arm is needed only when at least 2 fixes pass and clA contains a failing one. The case where MODE 1 passes and MODE 2 fails, with another fix passing, is included. If only one fix passes, the combination is its single arm, and if none pass nothing ships. Section 9.6 step 4 does mention "the combination re-run if needed", so the draft plans it; it just does not count it. Size: 146 runs + 4 shards.
(c) The flip identity runs and the event-attribution wave are not planned-but-uncounted. The draft does not plan them at all: section 7 re-runs only the suite at the flip, and G4's per-death attribution reads the CRN arms already in the budget. The flip check is a real planning gap, since project precedent is to verify a flip. The attribution wave is a contingency; the draft does not size it.
FIX(verified): Rewrite 9.5 and D-5 as a range, with every assumption named:
- BEST CASE: 12 at once, per-run wall unchanged. 524 stock runs x 516 s plus 511 CRN runs x 516 s x 1.22, divided by 12, is about 13.7 h. Add the rbgate at 7 arms x about 9.8 k shard-seconds, +1.6 h, for about 15 h in total (about 13 h without clSB).
- MEASURED-THROUGHPUT CASE: the only recorded 360-step wave of this code (_uh_wave.log, 72.8 s per run at 7.1 runs at once, throttled by commit) gives about 23 h for the runs plus 2.7 h of rbgate, about 26 h in total.
State that commit is no longer expected to bind (0.86 GB per sim; 23 GB free) but that no wave of this code at 360 steps has run above 8 at once.

Make stage 1 (clB + clC, 26 runs) run 12 at once. Re-project the budget from its measured per-run wall, split by harness, and report the projection before stage 2 starts.

List the conditional work separately, each with its trigger and size:
- (i) Combination re-run: 146 runs + 4 rb shards, about 2-3.5 h. Trigger: at least 2 fixes pass and clA contains a failing one, including MODE 2 failing while MODE 1 passes.

VERIFIER: The finding holds against the recorded data. It actually understates the problem: on U30 stock, mode 1 is predicted to make the M1-primary count go UP, not stay flat.

1. The replay's F1 rule is mode 1. In `fs_counterfactual.py:64-65` it completes on any boundary cell, and stalled is counted at :141 as `n > leg["dist"] + 5`, where dist is measured to the fixed `exit_target` (:42). That is the draft's M1-primary. The per-arm tables:
   - `cf_uhD.txt`: base 102 legs, 812 steps, 5 stalled; F1 747 steps, 5 stalled.
   - `cf_ugKD.txt`: 755/3 -> 754/3.
   - `cf_ugKC.txt`: 687/4 -> 686/4.

2. I ran a copy of the replay with -v in my scratch dir (offline, recorded JSON only, no writes). For uhD `east_half_1433805104`: s0=206, rec=76, dist=7, F1=13. 13 > 7+5 = 12, so the draft's showcase leg (:261, "completion ~219 instead of 282") is still stalled on the primary metric, by one step.    - Mode 1 un-stalls only e/h/909 x2 on the primary metric.
   - 909 is in neither C13 (101-505) nor U30.
   - The C13 ON legs mode 1 touches save 0-1 steps.

3. New evidence the reviewer missed. `m1_broken.py` scans broken legs in the ug*/uh* files, walking ff_steps to the first boundary cell. In `_ffr_uhD_east_half_1291443119.json`:
   - The dropped carry is ff_unit_1 / victim_0, s0=84, pickup (41,33), d=8.
   - At the end of step 107 the carrier stands on (49,38), a boundary cell, still exiting and en_route. It drops at 148 and the victim dies at 150.
   - Under mode 1 it completes at 108, a 24-step leg. 24 > 13, so the new leg is stalled on the primary metric, and on the secondary one too (d' = 13, and 24 > 18).
   - uhY is identical. ugD/ugY/ugC/uhC show the same carry.
   - With fire as recorded, U30 stock goes from 5 stalled legs to 6: 1433805104 stays stalled, and the converted drop adds one.
   - M1 (draft :754-756) counts only (unit, victim) legs that end in a completion. A clC carry that broke is invisible there. When a feature arm turns it into a slow rescue, it enters the count as a new stall.

   So clE1 is predicted to fail G2's "not higher in any single set" (:806-808) precisely because it saved a victim.
   - The same artefact hits clA through the HOLD conversions. The draft's hold-alone replays (:466-468) have uhD completing at 163, a 79-step leg, and fmDRY 101 at 150 from s0 100, a 50-step leg against d=14. Both would count as stalled.
   - The draft's line "HOLD / SERVED arms ... not expected to move stalls" (:814-815) is therefore wrong for M1 as defined.
   - Nothing in 2.2 or 9.4 pre-registers any of this. The inert rule at :822-825 covers only HOLD and SERVED.

4. Two corrections to the reviewer's framing:
   - Mode 1 is not merely "inert" on the metric. It is predicted to raise it, and the rise comes from a rescue.
   - The reviewer's fix proposes a relaxed mechanism line as mode 1's pass criterion. That would replace the brief's gate ("stalled exit legs FALL") with something else, which the brief does not allow. The right fix is to repair the metric and pre-register the prediction, not to relax the gate.

Severity medium: as written, the report would record "mode 1 raised stalls / failed G2" where the recorded fire says it turned a death into a rescue. The same artefact would also count clA's conversions against it.

No command touched `outputs/_firemech_rewound_20260914`. The only listing was a non-recursive `os.listdir` of outputs/, which skipped that name, plus reads of named `_ffr_*` files.
FIX(verified): (a) Fix M1 so a conversion cannot count as harm (section 9.3 M1, used by G2 for every MODE arm and for clA). Choose one rule and pre-register it:
  - either count every non-horizon broken exit leg as STALLED in the arm where it broke ("stalled-or-broken"). A carry that never reached the edge is at least as bad as a stall, so a broken-to-slow-completion conversion is neutral and a fast conversion is a gain;
  - or compute G2's stall count only over legs completed in BOTH arms (matched as in the none-longer rule), and report clC-broken legs that the feature arm completes separately under M3 as conversions.

[mode12] D1-02 medium sec 8.4 (pre-wave kernel check) / 2.3.1 VERDICT CONFIRMED
CLAIM: The pre-wave check as specified ('replaces fs_rules.bfs_first_step with the IMPLEMENTED agents.exit_leg_first_step') can pass without testing anything. fs_counterfactual binds the name at import, so rebinding fs_rules.bfs_first_step after that import changes nothing, and the replay still runs its own kernel. The pass condition ('reproduce the numbers exactly') is also exactly what that no-op produces. A mistranscribed kernel would then go into a ~1,000-run wave, the same trap as 'a passing prototype validated the rule, not the transcription'. The check also feeds the replay's own predicates, so it never exercises the model-side composition (helpers + memo), which is covered only by one constructed board.
EVIDENCE: outputs/_cl_replay/fs_counterfactual.py:18 'from fs_rules import move_toward, bfs_first_step, on_boundary, md'; :75 'nxt = bfs_first_step(env, pos, k)' resolves in fs_counterfactual's own namespace. Draft :700-708; _cl_replay/README.txt 'imports the IMPLEMENTED kernel in place of fs_rules.bfs_first_step'. The signatures differ, (env,pos,k,strict) vs (start, passable, is_boundary, out_of_bounds), so an adapter is needed anyway. Draft :708-709: 'Plus a constructed-model test ... on the same board' (singular).
FIX(reviewer): Rebind the consumer: fs_counterfactual.bfs_first_step = adapter (or rebind fs_rules before importing fs_counterfactual), and assert fs_counterfactual.bfs_first_step is adapter before any run. Make a canary mandatory first: a mutated kernel (goal test before the passable filter, or reversed neighbour order) MUST change the census row, and only then must the real kernel reproduce 1643 / 20 / 2 / 12 / 3 / 59 shorter / 0 longer / 0 dead and uhD 588. Replace the single constructed board with a randomized differential test between Firefighter._exit_leg_step on a fake grid and exit_leg_first_step fed replay-style predicates. Use thousands of boards and include burning and smoky starts, corners, all-smoky edges and tied boundary neighbours.
VERIFIER: The main claim holds, and I checked it by running it. I copied fs_env.py, fs_rules.py and fs_counterfactual.py verbatim into scratch/review/verify_D1-02 and wrote check_binding.py. It reads only the recorded JSON named in list_census.txt and list_uhD.txt; no simulation ran.

1. The rebind the draft describes does nothing. fs_counterfactual.py:18 runs `from fs_rules import ... bfs_first_step`, and replay() at :75 resolves the name in fs_counterfactual's own globals. If the driver imports fs_counterfactual and then sets fs_rules.bfs_first_step = adapter, `fs_counterfactual.bfs_first_step is adapter` is False and the adapter is called 0 times. The run still prints exactly the pre-registered numbers: census F5 212 legs / 1643 steps / 20 stalled / adj 12 / smoke 3 / 59 shorter / 0 longer / 0 dead, and uhD 588 / 2 / 0 / 0 / 5 / 0. So under the pass condition in draft :700-706, testing nothing reads as a PASS. If the patch is applied before fs_counterfactual is imported, the adapter is bound and called 1431 times on the census (486 on uhD). The draft (:701-703, "replaces fs_rules.bfs_first_step") and _cl_replay/README.txt ("in place of fs_rules.bfs_first_step") both name the wrong binding and give no ordering rule and no bound-check.

2. The aggregate target barely reacts to a kernel error. This is new evidence the reviewer did not give. I tried two mutants. With goal-before-filter, the census reads 1608/14 (adj 31/6) and uhD 584. With neighbour order reversed, the census reads 1641/24 (adj 0/0), but uhD stays at exactly 588/2/0/0/5/0 even though 4 uhD legs end differently. A tie-order transcription error therefore passes the "uhD 588" line. The only thing catching it is a 2-step change in the census total, which is also what nearby non-kernel differences could produce.

3. The adapter is required anyway. The signatures are exit_leg_first_step(start, passable, is_boundary, out_of_bounds) (draft :293) and bfs_first_step(env, pos, k, strict) (fs_rules.py:82).

4. The reviewer overstates one point. The model-side composition is not covered only by one board: draft 8.2 (:663-667) also lists a corridor test, a refused smoky or fire-adjacent goal, a bit-for-bit fallback, determinism, no RNG and no stored state. The real gaps are narrower. None of those tests compares Firefighter._exit_leg_step with the replay kernel. None pins the start-cell exemption from 2.3.1 (a carrier on a smoky, fire-adjacent or burning cell must still search). If that exemption lived in the wrapper rather than the kernel, the replay-fed check could not see it. The predicate semantics themselves do match (agents.py:1912-1946 against fs_env.py:102-112: out-of-bounds counts as not burning, and burning is filtered before smoke), so the risk is in the composition, not the definitions. A randomized differential of about 1,000 boards is cheap and good. Calling for "thousands" is a preference; the number is not the substance of the fix.

5. The reviewer's canary works on the census row, since both mutants change it, but it is weaker than a per-call comparison and fails on uhD. So the fix below should be the primary check, with the canary as a secondary guard.

Quarantine: no command listed, read or traversed outputs/_firemech_rewound_20260914. The replay loads explicit file names and asserts the quarantine name is absent, and I ran Python with -B, so no __pycache__ was written in the repo (confirmed 0).
FIX(verified): Rewrite section 8.4 (and the matching sentence in outputs/_cl_replay/README.txt) as follows.

(a) BINDING. The driver binds the adapter in the consumer module: `import fs_counterfactual as fc; fc.bfs_first_step = adapter`, or patch fs_rules before fs_counterfactual is first imported. It then asserts `fc.bfs_first_step is adapter` before any leg runs. The adapter is `lambda env,pos,k,strict=True: agents.exit_leg_first_step(pos, passable=lambda n: not env.burning(n,k) and not env.adj(n,k) and not env.smoky(n,k), is_boundary=on_boundary, out_of_bounds=lambda c: not inb(c))`.

(b) PER-CALL DIFFERENTIAL AS THE PRIMARY CHECK. The adapter calls both fs_rules' original bfs_first_step and the implemented kernel on every invocation, asserts the two cells are identical, and counts calls. PASS requires 1431 calls on list_census.txt and 486 on list_uhD.txt (F_GUESS=8, F5 rule only), with 0 mismatches. It also requires per-leg (n, end) equality with the original. Only after that must the aggregate row reproduce 1643 / 20 / 2 (to the reached cell) / 12 / 3 / 59 shorter / 0 longer / 0 dead, and uhD 588. A call count of 0, or any count different from the pre-registered one, is a STOP.

(c) CANARY, SECONDARY. Before the real run, the same driver is run with a mutated kernel: goal test before the clean filter must give 1608/14 and adj 31. It must report mismatches and a changed census row, which proves the harness is wired in. Do not rely on uhD for this: a reversed neighbour order leaves uhD at 588/2/0/0/5/0.

(d) MODEL-SIDE COMPOSITION. Replace "a constructed-model test ... on the same board" with a seeded randomized differential of at least about 1,000 boards. Each board uses a fake grid with Fire agents set burning or smoky, and the test compares the cell chosen by Firefighter._exit_leg_step with exit_leg_first_step fed the replay-style predicates computed from the same board. The boards must include burning, smoky and fire-adjacent start cells (the 2.3.1 start exemption, which no 8.2 test pins today), corners, all-smoky and all-fire-adjacent edges (the fallback path must equal _move_toward bit-for-bit), tied equidistant boundary cells, and a start that is already on the boundary (the completion must fire before any search). Also add the start-exemption case to the 8.2 unit-test list.

VERIFIER: I tried to refute each part of D1-04 against HEAD 6160438 (`git status --porcelain -uno` is empty). None of it fell, so the finding stands at medium.

1. **No instrument records path steps versus fallback steps.**
   - The harnesses never touch `_last_move_tier` or `movement_reason`: a grep of `_ffr_harness.py` and `_fm2_probe_harness.py` finds nothing.
   - The `ff_steps` row has six fields and no tier (wf1_results.txt:363).
   - The firefighter transition log fires only when `transition_key` changes (agents.py:1645). For a firefighter that key is just the notable category (movement_explainability.py:35-45), and all three exiting categories map to "exiting" (lines 28-31). The harness does not save this log anyway.

2. **The planned substitute cannot settle anything.** The draft's only fallback-related measurement is "steps NOT the kernel's choice (post-wave check against burn_intervals + reconstructed smoke)" (draft :759-761).
   - Smoke is not recorded per cell (draft :781-782).
   - The reconstruction has 115 smoke errors (fp 32, fn 83), all on cells with guessed fuel (wf1_results.txt:39).
   - A mismatch could come from reconstruction error, the fallback, or a real implementation deviation. That one number cannot clear the implementation or convict it.
   - The draft pre-registers no live fallback count, even though its own replay estimate exists (4/212 legs, 13/1643 advances, draft :333). So Part 3 cannot say how many carriers had no path, or what happened to them.
   - D-3 asks that a longer leg on a diverged fire be reported "with its first differing step and cause". Without the path/fallback record that cause cannot be established.
   - The 8.4 constructed-board test covers the kernel before the wave, but not live behaviour.

3. **The stale-tier point is real.**
   - The no-neighbour branch at agents.py:2411-2413 returns before `_last_move_tier` is written at 2457.
   - At HOLD 0 in mode 2, the drop step then records "exiting_with_victim" with a leftover tier (agents.py:1848-1853), possibly 5.
   - The draft resets the tier to 0 only on the HOLD 1 branch (draft :393).
   - A probe that reads `_last_move_tier` would therefore misclassify the drop step.

4. **The label still names the fixed exit cell.** The mode-2 path-step record still reads "carrying rescued victim to boundary {self.exit_target}" (agents.py:1850). The draft changes only the tier (:288).

5. **The reviewer's one-sided invariant is exact.**
   - Fire agents are added to the schedule first (set_fire_agents at wildfire_model.py:182, before the UAVs at :244).
   - Mesa 1.2.1 `SimultaneousActivation` runs `advance()` in insertion order (time.py:142-149).
   - The only write that sets a cell burning is `Fire.advance` (agents.py:125, confirmed by grep). After it, only firefighter extinguish writes run, and they only remove fire.
   - So post-step burning is a subset of the burning each unit saw when it decided. A path step's cell must be neither burning nor 4-adjacent to a burning cell in `burn_intervals` at that step.
   - This is a necessary condition only: it checks neither smoke nor that the search picked a shortest path.

**Refinements to the reviewer's fix:**
- **Record the implementation's own outcome, not `_last_move_tier`.** The tier is stale after the no-neighbour return.
- **Share one instrument across arms.** A sidecar wrapper run only on the mode-2 arms would give those arms a different entry point from clC. Either run it on every arm, or prove it passes values through unchanged. Simpler: log in the model and have the harness emit the key conditionally, as with firefight_log (_ffr_harness.py:806-834). The CRN harness runs `_ffr_harness` unchanged via runpy (_fm2_probe_harness.py:196), so both instruments pick it up, and mode-0 JSON is unchanged.
- **The goal cell is optional.** Recording it needs the kernel to return `(first_step, goal)`, which changes the pure signature the 8.4 replay driver imports. `completions[].pos` already gives the cell reached.

WHAT HOLDS (from the code):
- Custody does suspend the victim's flee. Victim guard G4 at agents.py:1325-1326 returns while the victim is in custody.
- The carrier's survival retreat is also off while it is exiting (agents.py:1973, `or self.exiting`).
- So release trades the victim's flee plus the ex-carrier's retreat and firefighting against continued custody. For SURVIVAL, "writing the victim off cannot help it" is unproven. For RESCUE the sentence is true: the victim is cancelled, finalize refuses it, and no unit is dispatched to it again.
- The gate is G3 rescued and G4 ff_deaths. The SN side-effects list (draft:536-538) leaves out "dead". But "dead" IS measured per arm in M5 (draft:772-775), and the brief does not gate it.

WHAT DOES NOT HOLD:
1. The per-step death rule favours custody, not release.
   - A victim dies iff its own cell burns at the sweep (wildfire_model.py:5208-5240).
   - A carrier always moves to some non-burning neighbour if one exists: `scored` holds every non-burning neighbour, and tier 4 takes the minimum over all of them (agents.py:2394-2451). A carried victim therefore dies only on full enclosure with its own cell burning.
   - A released victim escapes a burning cell only to a non-burning neighbour within 6 cells of its leash anchor (agents.py:1345-1375). That is a stricter condition.
   - The two moves differ only in trajectory; neither rule dominates.
2. The record points the other way.
   - Of the 9 releases, 5 victims died after release. In 4 of those the released carrier lived far longer: east/def/101 victim died 117, carrier died 195; east/half/606 victim died 129, carrier died 210; east/half/1010 and west/half/303 carriers alive at the end (wf1_results:800-808).
   - dfpB west/half/505 (one of the finding's two "fled" survivors) was written off at step 104 on (6,25). It was then 6 cells from its exit (0,25), walking straight west on clean cells (neighbour count 0/4 at steps 97-104). The victim sat on (6,25) until 188 and fled only from 189. Custody predicts a completion near step 110, a RESCUE rather than a death.
   - f2cDRY was likewise 3 cells from its exit when written off.
3. The d4off 1323590814 case is not an adverse shape.
   - The idle ex-carrier died at (48,13) at step 138 because it stood still on (48,13) through steps 136-137, which is idle behaviour.
   - I replayed the carrying rule on the recorded burn_intervals, with the exit at (49,15) (pickup (43,15)). At 135 its own cell ignites and (47,13) is the only non-burning neighbour, so it moves there. It then goes to (48,13) at 136 by tier 4, to (48,14) at 137, and at 138 to (49,14), a non-burning boundary cell, where it is alive.
   - With smoke on (48,13), the alternative path is (47,12) -> (47,13) -> (47,14), and it is alive at 138 there too.
   - Either way, with HOLD 0 the enclosure drop at 139 releases the victim just as in the real run. Smoke is not recorded per cell, so this replay is approximate, but custody does not produce the claimed death.
4. Fix item 4 is already covered: M7 is computed per arm, so clSN gets its own carrying-leg exposure.
5. Fix item 2's per-death attribution asks for more than the brief requires. A report line is enough.

Quarantine: no contact. I read only the named files outputs/_ffr_d4off_east_half_1323590814.json, outputs/_ffr_dfpB_west_half_505.json and outputs/_ffr_harness.py, plus agents.py and wildfire_model.py. ls touched two other named files. There was no recursive search.
FIX(verified): Downgrade to LOW. Make two edits and nothing more.

"Writing the victim off cannot get it rescued. It is cancelled, finalize refuses it, no unit is ever dispatched to it again, and none could reach it sooner than the co-located carrier. It is not a survival guarantee either way. Release restores the victim's flee (agents.py:1325-1326) and the ex-carrier's retreat (1973) and firefighting. Custody ties the victim's survival to the carrier's move rule: only non-burning cells, no leash, death only on full enclosure with its own cell burning. A BETTER CHECK THE FINDING MISSES
- SERVED touches only internal streak state (_unreachable_geo_streak, which is not recorded) until a marking it prevents. reachable_cells feeds only geo_reachable (:5014-5076). The in_custody flag is read only by unreachable_escape_victims.
- So there is an exact, cheap footprint invariant. clSN must equal clC value for value on every run with no custody marking in clC. On the other runs, its first differing step must be the clC custody-marking step. clSB is the same, anchored at clC geographically_isolated markings.
- This makes every SN/SB movement in M6, M8 or never_detected belong to the D3 population by construction. It needs no reconstruction of the two predicates.

VERIFIER: The finding's main evidence is wrong. Two smaller points in it are right but low severity.

1. REFUTED: "15 of the 19 were undetected candidates, so rung 2 mostly relabels them never_detected at 210." The reviewer took status from victim_steps, and that field is not a detection flag. A detected victim with no free unit keeps its managed status "candidate". _sync_victim_agent_status (wildfire_model.py:5568-5573) first sets marker.status = "confirmed" when state.confirmed is true, then overwrites it with state.status ("candidate"). The harness records victim_steps from that marker (outputs/_ffr_harness.py:536-542).

Detection is deterministic: _detect_victims_in_uav_radius (wildfire_model.py:1326-1406) confirms any live, non-terminal victim within UAV_OBSERVATION_RADIUS 8 (cfv:706). It runs in both cycles (adaptation_manager.py:101 and :171). I tested a radius-8 predictor on every 4th _ffr_*.json file:
- 83 of 83 never_detected-marked victims had no UAV within 8 before step 210;
- 8 of 8 victims still "candidate" at the end with no escape marking had had UAV contact. Their undetected streak was reset, so they had been confirmed.

Applied to the 15 co-marked "candidates", 14 had UAV contact before the marking step:
- dimA v1@108, v2@72;
- dimB v1@107, v2@68;
- f2cDRY v2@163, v3@118;
- dimfreshbase 606 v1@55, v3@30;
- 1010 v2@20;
- d4off v1@64, v3@24;
- dfpCB 303 v0@53, v1@20;
- dfpB 505 v1@20.

Only 1010 v1 was truly undetected. So 18 of 19 co-marked victims were confirmed and needy. The planner needy filter (rescue_planner.py:550) and _try_dispatch_unresolved_confirmed_victims (wildfire_model.py:2778-2784) read state.confirmed, not the status label. None of the 18 would be relabelled never_detected: that marking needs an unbroken 210-step streak, and every recorded never_detected mark is at step 210 with streak 210. The proposed "15/4 split" and the "never_detected +k, rescued +0" pre-registration would put a label artefact into the design.

2. PARTLY RIGHT, low: "by whom" and "inert at MODE 0". In all 9 cases the only live unit is the carrier. So rung 2 pays off only if the carry completes, the unit comes back from its 3-5 step absence (cfv:119-120), and a recycle dispatch follows. The draft never says this. But "clSB vs clSN about inert" does not follow:
- 5 of 9 carriers were alive at the horizon (f2cDRY, 1010, f2b404s12, dfpCB, dfpB).
- The f2cDRY carrier was moving straight east (168-177) and was 3 cells from its exit when marked.
- In its CRN sibling f2cOFF, which has the same fire, co-marked victim_2 was assigned at 187 to the lone surviving unit.
- The horizon is 360, not 240.

3. REFUTED: "no arm isolates rung 2". Draft section 4.3 says "rung 2 vs rung 1 attributes it alone", which is clSB vs clSN. "Value appears only with MODE on" has it backwards. Co-marking needs a solo carry of at least 30 steps. MODE 2 removes most of those, so clA is where rung 2 has the least to act on. A clA(S1)/clA(S2) pair would spend 146 runs on a population close to empty. The one valid part: clA's "SERVED 1 (or 2)" should be pinned to the D-1 answer before any wave runs.

The checks reject any status outside {rescued, dead, unreachable, cancelled} (test_unresolved_victim_coverage.py:272-279, :304, :313; test_firefighter_fire_avoidance.py:237-238, :255-256):
- (a) a carry or hold still running at 300 leaves its victim "assigned" (SERVED>=1, HOLD 1);
- (b) at rung 2, a confirmed non-carried victim whose streak was reset while a carrier was on the grid is still "confirmed" (rescue_planner.py:497-500);
- (c) at SERVED 1 / HOLD 0, a carry kept past pickup+29 and then dropped leaves its victim "confirmed";
- (d) any switch can make the seed-42 run diverge; model.step() is driven by 22 test files.
If a sim run fails one of these tests, read managed_victims at step 300 to find the route. A victim still legitimately in custody at 300 is a question about the test's expectation, and goes to the maintainer. It is not an automatic pass.

(6) Keep the flip-commit run as confirmation only. Its failing set must equal the matching sim set minus the pins the flip commit updates. On any mismatch, revert the flip.
